<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 145. PUBLIC HEALTH PROVISIONS</h2>
      
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.01"><b>145.01</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.071"><b>145.071</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.001">15.001</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.075">
        <h1 class="shn">145.075 INJUNCTIVE RELIEF BROUGHT BY COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <p>In addition to any other remedy provided by law, the commissioner may in the commissioner's own name bring an action in the court of appropriate jurisdiction to enjoin any violation of a statute or rule which the commissioner is empowered to enforce or adopt, or to enjoin as a public health nuisance any activity or failure to act that adversely affects the public health.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.075.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/762/#laws.0.7.0">1978 c 762 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.18.0">1987 c 309 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.08"><b>145.08</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.085"><b>145.085</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.09"><b>145.09</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/45/#laws.0.73.0">1965 c 45 s 73</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.10"><b>145.10</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.11"><b>145.11</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.12"><b>145.12</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.123"><b>145.123</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.125"><b>145.125</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.13"><b>145.13</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/357/#laws.0.22.0">1980 c 357 s 22</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ALZHEIMER'S AND OTHER DEMENTIA DISEASES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.131">
        <h1 class="shn">145.131 FINDINGS AND PURPOSE.</h1>
        <p>The legislature finds that Alzheimer's and other dementia diseases occur in recipients of medical assistance. The costs the state pays in terms of human suffering, lost productivity, and medical assistance expenditures are enormous. The legislature also finds that research for the identification, cause, cure, and prevention of Alzheimer's and other dementia diseases requires autopsies and pathological studies of suspected victims. Expenses for autopsies and pathological studies are not provided for recipients of medical assistance.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.131.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/9/#laws.2.14.0">1Sp1985 c 9 art 2 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.132"><b>145.132</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/192/#laws.4.3.0">2014 c 192 art 4 s 3</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DEAD BODIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.135">
        <h1 class="shn">145.135 UNIFORM DETERMINATION OF DEATH ACT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.135.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as the "Uniform Determination of Death Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.135.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Determination of death.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual is dead if the individual sustains irreversible cessation of:</p>
          <p>(1) circulatory and respiratory functions; or</p>
          <p>(2) all functions of the entire brain, including the brain stem.</p>
          <p>A determination of death must be made in accordance with generally accepted medical standards.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.135.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/93/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 93 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.14"><b>145.14</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.47.0">1997 c 215 s 47</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.15"><b>145.15</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.47.0">1997 c 215 s 47</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.16"><b>145.16</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.47.0">1997 c 215 s 47</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.161">
        <h1 class="shn">145.161 DISSECTION; WHEN PERMITTED.</h1>
        <p>The right to dissect the dead body of a human being shall be limited to: (a) cases specially provided by statute, or by the direction or will of the deceased; (b) cases where a coroner is authorized to hold an inquest upon the body, and then only so far as the coroner may authorize dissection; (c) cases where the spouse shall authorize dissection for the purpose of ascertaining the cause of death, and then only to the extent so authorized; and (d) cases where one of the next of kin, charged by law with the duty of burial, shall authorize dissection for the purpose of ascertaining the cause of death and then only to the extent so authorized, provided no dissection shall be performed pursuant to this clause if there is objection by anyone of such next of kin. Every person who shall make, cause or procure to be made, any dissection of the body of a human being, except as hereinbefore provided, shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.161.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/98">10227</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/98">RL s 4975</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/220/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 220 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/101/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 101 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.162"><b>145.162</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.47.0">1997 c 215 s 47</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.1621">
        <h1 class="shn">145.1621 DISPOSITION OF ABORTED OR MISCARRIED FETUSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.1621.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of this section is to protect the public health and welfare by providing for the dignified and sanitary disposition of the remains of aborted or miscarried human fetuses in a uniform manner and to declare violations of this section to be a public nuisance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.1621.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition; remains of a human fetus.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the term "remains of a human fetus" means the remains of the dead offspring of a human being that has reached a stage of development so that there are cartilaginous structures, fetal or skeletal parts after an abortion or miscarriage, whether or not the remains have been obtained by induced, spontaneous, or accidental means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.1621.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Regulation of disposal.</span></h2>
          <p>Remains of a human fetus resulting from an abortion or miscarriage, induced or occurring accidentally or spontaneously at a hospital, clinic, or medical facility must be deposited or disposed of in this state only at the place and in the manner provided by this section or, if not possible, as directed by the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.1621.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Disposition; tests.</span></h2>
          <p>Hospitals, clinics, and medical facilities in which abortions are induced or occur spontaneously or accidentally and laboratories to which the remains of human fetuses are delivered must provide for the disposal of the remains by cremation, interment by burial, or in a manner directed by the commissioner of health. The hospital, clinic, medical facility, or laboratory may complete laboratory tests necessary for the health of the woman or her future offspring or for purposes of a criminal investigation or determination of parentage prior to disposing of the remains.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.1621.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Violation; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Failure to comply with this section constitutes a public nuisance. A person, firm, or corporation failing to comply with this section is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.1621.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exclusions.</span></h2>
          <p>To comply with this section, a religious service or ceremony is not required as part of the disposition of the remains of a human fetus, and no discussion of the method of disposition is required with the woman obtaining an induced abortion.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.1621.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/238/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 238 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.1622">
        <h1 class="shn">145.1622 POLICY FOR NOTIFICATION OF DISPOSITION OPTIONS.</h1>
        <p class="in">Hospitals, clinics, and medical facilities must have in place by January 15, 2009, a policy for informing a woman of available options for fetal disposition when the woman experiences a miscarriage or is expected to experience a miscarriage.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.1622.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/326/#laws.1.4.0">2008 c 326 art 1 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.163"><b>145.163</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.47.0">1997 c 215 s 47</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.17"><b>145.17</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.18"><b>145.18</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.19"><b>145.19</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.20"><b>145.20</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.21"><b>145.21</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.22"><b>145.22</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.23"><b>145.23</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.24"><b>145.24</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.47.0">1997 c 215 s 47</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.267">
        <h1 class="shn">145.267 FETAL ALCOHOL SPECTRUM DISORDERS PREVENTION GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of health shall award a grant to a statewide organization that focuses solely on prevention of and intervention with fetal alcohol spectrum disorders. The grant recipient must make subgrants to eligible regional collaboratives in rural and urban areas of the state for the purposes specified in paragraph (c).</p>
        <p>(b) "Eligible regional collaboratives" means a partnership between at least one local government or Tribal government and at least one community-based organization and, where available, a family home visiting program. For purposes of this paragraph, a local government includes a county or a multicounty organization, a county-based purchasing entity, or a community health board.</p>
        <p>(c) Eligible regional collaboratives must use subgrant funds to reduce the incidence of fetal alcohol spectrum disorders and other prenatal drug-related effects in children in Minnesota by identifying and serving pregnant women suspected of or known to use or abuse alcohol or other drugs. Eligible regional collaboratives must provide intensive services to women with substance use disorder to increase positive birth outcomes.</p>
        <p>(d) An eligible regional collaborative that receives a subgrant under this section must report to the grant recipient by January 15 of each year on the services and programs funded by the subgrant. The report must include measurable outcomes for the previous year, including the number of pregnant women served and the number of toxin-free babies born. The grant recipient must compile the information in the subgrant reports and submit a summary report to the commissioner of health by February 15 of each year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.267.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.1.61.0">2022 c 98 art 1 s 61</a>; art 4 s 51</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">HOSPITAL RECORDS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.30">
        <h1 class="shn">145.30 SUPERINTENDENT OF HOSPITALS TO TRANSFER RECORDS.</h1>
        <p>The superintendent or other chief administrative officer of any public or private hospital, by and with the consent and approval of its board of directors or other governing body, is authorized to transfer and record, or cause to be transferred and recorded, upon photographic film, electronic image, or other state-of-the-art electronic preservation technology of convenient size for the preservation thereof as evidence, any or all of the original files and records of any such hospital dealing with the case history, physical examination, and daily hospital records of the individual patients thereof, including any miscellaneous documents, papers, and correspondence in connection therewith.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/229/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 229 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/228/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 228 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.31">
        <h1 class="shn">145.31 COPIES TO BE USED AS EVIDENCE.</h1>
        <p>Upon the transferring and recording of any such original hospital files and records in the manner hereinbefore provided, such photographic film, electronic image, or other state-of-the-art electronic preservation technology records thereof shall have the same force and effect, when offered in evidence in any proceeding in this state, as the original records from which the same were so transferred and recorded, and any copy made therefrom, when duly certified in writing, attached thereto, by the officer or employee of such hospital in charge of the records, to be such correct and complete copy thereof, shall be admitted and received in evidence, without further foundation, in any proceeding in this state with the same force and effect as the original record of such hospital from which such copy was originally made, whether the original is in existence or not.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/229/#laws.0.2.0">1941 c 229 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/231/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 231 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/228/#laws.0.2.0">2008 c 228 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.32">
        <h1 class="shn">145.32 OLD RECORDS MAY BE DESTROYED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Hospital records.</span></h2>
          <p>The superintendent or other chief administrative officer of any public or private hospital, by and with the consent and approval of the board of directors or other governing body of the hospital, may divest the files and records of that hospital of any individual case records and, with that consent and approval, may destroy the records. The records shall first have been transferred and recorded as authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.30">145.30</a>.</p>
          <p>Portions of individual hospital medical records that comprise an individual permanent medical record, as defined by the commissioner of health, shall be retained as authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.30">145.30</a>. Other portions of the individual medical record, including any miscellaneous documents, papers, and correspondence in connection with them, may be divested and destroyed after seven years without transfer to photographic film, electronic image, or other state-of-the-art electronic preservation technology.</p>
          <p>All portions of individual hospital medical records of minors shall be maintained for seven years or until the individual reaches the age of majority, whichever occurs last, at which time the individual may request that the patient's hospital records be destroyed, unless the hospital is required to retain the records as part of the individual's permanent medical record as defined in accordance with subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall be construed to prohibit the retention of hospital medical records beyond the periods described in this section. Nor shall anything in this section be construed to prohibit patient access to hospital medical records as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.291">144.291</a> to 144.298.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Individual permanent medical record.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health shall define by rule the term "individual permanent medical record" by enumerating the specific types of records or other information that, at a minimum, must be maintained on a permanent basis by the hospital.</p>
          <p>(b) "Individual permanent medical record" includes outpatient diagnostic and laboratory test results.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/229/#laws.0.3.0">1941 c 229 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/231/#laws.0.2.0">1971 c 231 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/237/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 237 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/670/#laws.0.9.0">1988 c 670 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.10.15.0">2007 c 147 art 10 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/228/#laws.0.3.0">2008 c 228 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 3 s 36</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.33">
        <h1 class="shn">145.33 CONSTRUCTION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.30">145.30</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.33">145.33</a> shall not be construed as requiring any such public or private hospital to retain among its files and records, during the period hereinbefore specified or otherwise, any such individual hospital case records, miscellaneous documents, papers, or correspondence, except as the preservation and retention thereof is otherwise required by law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/229/#laws.0.4.0">1941 c 229 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.34"><b>145.34</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/202/#laws.0.42.0">1991 c 202 s 42</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.35"><b>145.35</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/202/#laws.0.42.0">1991 c 202 s 42</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EXPOSURE TO DISEASE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.36">
        <h1 class="shn">145.36 EXPOSING PERSON WITH CONTAGIOUS DISEASE.</h1>
        <p>Every person who shall willfully expose self or another affected with any contagious or infectious disease, in any public place or thoroughfare, except upon the person's necessary removal in a manner not dangerous to the public health, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/99">10270</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/99">RL s 5008</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.361">
        <h1 class="shn">145.361 LONG COVID AND RELATED CONDITIONS; ASSESSMENT AND MONITORING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.361.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Long COVID" means health problems that people experience four or more weeks after being infected with SARS-CoV-2, the virus that causes COVID-19. Long COVID is also called post-COVID conditions, long-haul COVID, chronic COVID, post-acute COVID, or post-acute sequelae of COVID-19 (PASC).</p>
          <p>(c) "Related conditions" means conditions associated with or sequelae of long COVID, including but not limited to myalgic encephalomyelitis/chronic fatigue syndrome (ME/CFS) and dysautonomia, and postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome (POTS).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.361.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health shall establish a program to conduct community assessments and epidemiologic investigations to monitor and address impacts of long COVID and related conditions. The purposes of these activities are to:</p>
          <p>(1) monitor trends in: incidence, prevalence, mortality, and health outcomes; changes in disability status, employment, and quality of life; and service needs of individuals with long COVID or related conditions and to detect potential public health problems, predict risks, and assist in investigating long COVID and related conditions health inequities;</p>
          <p>(2) more accurately target information and resources for communities and patients and their families;</p>
          <p>(3) inform health professionals and citizens about risks and early detection;</p>
          <p>(4) promote evidence-based practices around long COVID and related conditions prevention and management and to address public concerns and questions about long COVID and related conditions; and</p>
          <p>(5) research and track related conditions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.361.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Partnerships.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health shall, in consultation with health care professionals, the commissioner of human services, local public health entities, health insurers, employers, schools, survivors of long COVID or related conditions, and community organizations serving people at high risk of long COVID or related conditions, identify priority actions and activities to address the needs for communication, services, resources, tools, strategies, and policies to support survivors of long COVID or related conditions and their families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.361.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grants and contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health shall coordinate and collaborate with community and organizational partners to implement evidence-informed priority actions through community-based grants and contracts. The commissioner of health shall award grants and enter into contracts to organizations that serve communities disproportionately impacted by COVID-19, long COVID, or related conditions, including but not limited to rural and low-income areas, Black and African Americans, African immigrants, American Indians, Asian American-Pacific Islanders, Latino(a) communities, LGBTQ+ communities, and persons with living disabilities. Organizations may also address intersectionality within the groups. The commissioner shall award grants and award contracts to eligible organizations to plan, construct, and disseminate resources and information to support survivors of long COVID or related conditions, including caregivers, health care providers, ancillary health care workers, workplaces, schools, communities, and local and Tribal public health.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.361.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.50.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 50</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.365">
        <h1 class="shn">145.365 TRAFFICKING IN SKUNKS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.365.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to protect the public health and prevent human and domestic animal exposure to rabies, it shall be unlawful to:</p>
          <p>(1) import into or export out of this state any live skunk, for sale, barter, exchange or gift for any purpose whatsoever;</p>
          <p>(2) acquire, sell, barter, exchange, give, or purchase any live skunks.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.365.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of subdivision 1 do not apply to the importation, acquisition, or exportation of a skunk by a publicly or privately owned zoological park or circus or any other show where a skunk is exhibited but is not in physical contact with the public, or by scientific or educational institutions for research or educational purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.365.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/591/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 591 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.365.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Violation of subdivision 1 is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.365.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/591/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 591 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.41.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">INJURIOUS PRODUCTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.37">
        <h1 class="shn">145.37 MANUFACTURE OF CERTAIN PRODUCTS WHICH MAY BE INJURIOUS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Cement; waterproofing or curing products.</span></h2>
          <p>It shall be unlawful for any person to manufacture for sale or distribution within the state any product to be used in waterproofing or curing cement which product may be injurious to the skin or eyes of the user unless there is specified on the container of such product the chemical composition thereof, a warning of possible injurious effect, and the antidote in the event of injury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Violation of this section shall constitute a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/67/#laws.0.1.0">1957 c 67 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.38"><b>145.38</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/485/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 485 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.385"><b>145.385</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/485/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 485 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.39"><b>145.39</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/485/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 485 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.40"><b>145.40</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/485/#laws.0.3.0">1992 c 485 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.406"><b>145.406</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/239/#laws.3.25.0">1997 c 239 art 3 s 25</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.409">
        <h1 class="shn">145.409 REPRODUCTIVE HEALTH RIGHTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.409.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Short title.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as the "Protect Reproductive Options Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.409.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "reproductive health care" means health care offered, arranged, or furnished for the purpose of preventing pregnancy, terminating a pregnancy, managing pregnancy loss, or improving maternal health and birth outcomes. Reproductive health care includes, but is not limited to, contraception; sterilization; preconception care; maternity care; abortion care; family planning and fertility services; and counseling regarding reproductive health care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.409.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reproductive freedom.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Every individual has a fundamental right to make autonomous decisions about the individual's own reproductive health, including the fundamental right to use or refuse reproductive health care.</p>
          <p>(b) Every individual who becomes pregnant has a fundamental right to continue the pregnancy and give birth, or obtain an abortion, and to make autonomous decisions about how to exercise this fundamental right.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.409.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Right to reproductive freedom recognized.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Constitution establishes the principles of individual liberty, personal privacy, and equality. Such principles ensure the fundamental right to reproductive freedom.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.409.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Local unit of government limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>A local unit of government may not regulate an individual's ability to freely exercise the fundamental rights set forth in this section in a manner that is more restrictive than that set forth in this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.409.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/4/#laws.0.1.0">2023 c 4 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BLOOD DONATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.41">
        <h1 class="shn">145.41 BLOOD DONATIONS, AGE OF DONOR.</h1>
        <p>(a) Any person of the age of 17 years or over may donate blood in any voluntary and noncompensatory blood program without the necessity of obtaining parental permission or authorization.</p>
        <p>(b) A person who is 16 years of age may donate blood in a voluntary and noncompensatory blood program if the person obtains written permission from the person's parent or guardian.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/685/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 685 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/169/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 169 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/157/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 157 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ABORTION REGULATIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.411">
        <h1 class="shn">145.411 REGULATION OF ABORTIONS; DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Terms.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.411">145.411</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.415">145.415</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given to them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.411.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Hospital.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hospital" means an institution licensed by the state commissioner of health; adequately and properly staffed and equipped; providing services, facilities and beds for the reception and care of one or more nonrelated persons for a continuous period longer than 24 hours for diagnosis, treatment or care of illness, injury or pregnancy; and regularly providing clinical laboratory services, diagnostic x-ray services and treatment facilities for surgery, obstetrical care or other definitive medical treatment of similar extent. "Hospital" shall not include diagnostic or treatment centers, physicians' offices or clinics, or other facilities for the foster care of children licensed by the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.411.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.411.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Abortion.</span></h2>
          <p>"Abortion" includes an act, procedure or use of any instrument, medicine or drug which is supplied or prescribed for or administered to an individual with the intention of terminating, and which results in the termination of, pregnancy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.411.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/177/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 177 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.10.1.0">1998 c 407 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.51.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 51</a>,52</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.412"><b>145.412</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.145.413">
        <h1>145.413 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.413.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/14/#laws.2.2.0">2003 c 14 art 2 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.413.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.413.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4131">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4131 RECORDING AND REPORTING ABORTION DATA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4131.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Forms.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Within 90 days of July 1, 1998, the commissioner shall prepare a reporting form for use by physicians or facilities performing abortions. A copy of this section shall be attached to the form. A physician or facility performing an abortion shall obtain a form from the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The form shall require the following information:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the number of abortions performed by the physician in the previous calendar year, reported by month;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the method used for each abortion;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the approximate gestational age expressed in one of the following increments:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) less than nine weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) nine to ten weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) 11 to 12 weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) 13 to 15 weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(v) 16 to 20 weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(vi) 21 to 24 weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(vii) 25 to 30 weeks;</p>
          <p class="in">(viii) 31 to 36 weeks; or</p>
          <p class="in">(ix) 37 weeks to term;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the age of the woman at the time the abortion was performed;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) complications, if any, for each abortion and for the aftermath of each abortion. Space for a description of any complications shall be available on the form;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) the medical specialty of the physician performing the abortion; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) if the abortion was performed via telehealth, the facility code for the patient and the facility code for the physician.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4131.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Submission.</span></h2>
          <p>A physician performing an abortion or a facility at which an abortion is performed shall complete and submit the form to the commissioner no later than September 30 for abortions performed in the previous calendar year. The annual report to the commissioner shall include the methods used to dispose of fetal tissue and remains.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4131.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Additional reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall be construed to preclude the voluntary or required submission of other reports or forms regarding abortions.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4131.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.10.2.0">1998 c 407 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.8.43.0">2015 c 71 art 8 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.10.95.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 10 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.6.28.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 6 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.53.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 53</a>,54</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4132"><b>145.4132</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4133"><b>145.4133</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4134">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4134 COMMISSIONER'S PUBLIC REPORT.</h1>
        <p>(a) By December 31 of each year, the commissioner shall issue a public report providing statistics for the previous calendar year compiled from the data submitted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4131">145.4131</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4133">145.4133</a> and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4241">145.4241 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4249">145.4249</a>. Each report shall provide the statistics for all previous calendar years, adjusted to reflect any additional information from late or corrected reports. The commissioner shall ensure that none of the information included in the public reports can reasonably lead to identification of an individual having performed or having had an abortion. All data included on the forms under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4131">145.4131 </a>must be included in the public report, except that the commissioner shall maintain as confidential, data which alone or in combination may constitute information from which an individual having performed or having had an abortion may be identified using epidemiologic principles.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner may, by rules adopted under chapter 14, alter the submission dates established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4131">145.4131</a> for administrative convenience, fiscal savings, or other valid reason, provided that physicians or facilities submit the required information once each year and the commissioner issues a report once each year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4134.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.10.5.0">1998 c 407 art 10 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/14/#laws.2.1.0">2003 c 14 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.14.8.0">2022 c 98 art 14 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.55.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4135"><b>145.4135</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4136"><b>145.4136</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.414">
        <h1 class="shn">145.414 ABORTION NOT MANDATORY.</h1>
        <p>(a) No person and no hospital or institution shall be coerced, held liable or discriminated against in any manner because of a refusal to perform, accommodate, assist or submit to an abortion for any reason.</p>
        <p>(b) It is the policy of the state of Minnesota that no health plan company as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01#stat.62Q.01.4">62Q.01, subdivision 4</a>, or health care cooperative as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62R.04#stat.62R.04.2">62R.04, subdivision 2</a>, shall be required to provide or provide coverage for an abortion. No provision of this chapter; of chapter 62A, 62C, 62D, 62H, 62L, 62M, 62N, 62R, 62V, 64B, or of any other chapter; of Minnesota Rules; or of Laws 1995, chapter 234, shall be construed as requiring a health plan company as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01#stat.62Q.01.4">62Q.01, subdivision 4</a>, or a health care cooperative as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62R.04#stat.62R.04.2">62R.04, subdivision 2</a>, to provide or provide coverage for an abortion.</p>
        <p>(c) This section supersedes any provision of Laws 1995, chapter 234, or any act enacted prior to enactment of Laws 1995, chapter 234, that in any way limits or is inconsistent with this section. No provision of any act enacted subsequent to Laws 1995, chapter 234 shall be construed as in any way limiting or being inconsistent with this section, unless the act amends this section or expressly provides that it is intended to limit or be inconsistent with this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.414.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/177/#laws.0.4.0">1974 c 177 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.30.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.92.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 92</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE:</b> This section was found unconstitutional as it applies to public hospitals or institutions under <i>Hodgson v. Lawson</i>, 542 F.2d 1350 (8th Cir. 1976), but see also <i>Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization</i>, 142 S.Ct. 2228 (2022).</p>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.415"><b>145.415</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.416"><b>145.416</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.42">
        <h1 class="shn">145.42 ABORTIONS; NONLIABILITY FOR REFUSAL TO PERFORM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.42.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Damages.</span></h2>
          <p>No physician, nurse, or other person who refuses to perform or assist in the performance of an abortion, and no hospital that refuses to permit the performance of an abortion upon its premises, shall be liable to any person for damages allegedly arising from the refusal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.42.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Related actions.</span></h2>
          <p>No physician, nurse, or other person who refuses to perform or assist in the performance of an abortion shall, because of that refusal, be dismissed, suspended, demoted, or otherwise prejudiced or damaged by a hospital with which the person is affiliated or by which the person is employed.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/693/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 693 s 1</a>,2; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">USE OF HUMAN CONCEPTUS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.421">
        <h1 class="shn">145.421 HUMAN CONCEPTUS, LIVING; DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.421.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Terms.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.422">145.422</a>, the terms defined in this section shall have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.421.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Human conceptus.</span></h2>
          <p>"Human conceptus" means any human organism, conceived either in the human body or produced in an artificial environment other than the human body, from fertilization through the first 265 days thereafter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.421.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Living.</span></h2>
          <p>"Living," as defined for the sole purpose of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.422">145.422</a>, means the presence of evidence of life, such as movement, heart or respiratory activity, the presence of electroencephalographic or electrocardiographic activity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.421.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/562/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 562 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.422">
        <h1 class="shn">145.422 EXPERIMENTATION, RESEARCH OR SALE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.422.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Whoever uses or permits the use of a living human conceptus for any type of scientific, laboratory research or other experimentation except to protect the life or health of the conceptus, or except as herein provided, shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.422.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Permitted acts.</span></h2>
          <p>The use of a living human conceptus for research or experimentation which verifiable scientific evidence has shown to be harmless to the conceptus shall be permitted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.422.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalty; permitted payments.</span></h2>
          <p>Whoever buys or sells a living human conceptus or nonrenewable organ of the body is guilty of a gross misdemeanor. Nothing in this subdivision prohibits (1) the buying and selling of a cell culture line or lines taken from a nonliving human conceptus; (2) payments for reasonable expenses associated with the removal, storage, and transportation of a human organ, including payments made to or on behalf of a living organ donor for actual expenses such as medical costs, lost income, or travel expenses that are incurred as a direct result of the donation of the nonrenewable organ; or (3) financial assistance payments provided under insurance and Medicare reimbursement programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.422.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/562/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 562 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/475/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 475 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BORN ALIVE INFANTS<br>PROTECTION ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.423">
        <h1 class="shn">145.423 RECOGNITION OF INFANT WHO IS BORN ALIVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Recognition; care.</span></h2>
          <p>An infant who is born alive shall be fully recognized as a human person, and accorded immediate protection under the law. All reasonable measures consistent with good medical practice, including the compilation of appropriate medical records, shall be taken by the responsible medical personnel to care for the infant who is born alive.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.423.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.423.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/170/#laws.0.1.0">1976 c 170 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/215/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 215 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.8.44.0">2015 c 71 art 8 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.56.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 56,112</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4235"><b>145.4235</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.424">
        <h1 class="shn">145.424 PROHIBITION OF TORT ACTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.424.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Wrongful life action prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>No person shall maintain a cause of action or receive an award of damages on behalf of that person based on the claim that but for the negligent conduct of another, the person would have been aborted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.424.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Wrongful birth action prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>No person shall maintain a cause of action or receive an award of damages on the claim that but for the negligent conduct of another, a child would have been aborted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.424.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Failure or refusal to prevent a live birth.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall be construed to preclude a cause of action for intentional or negligent malpractice or any other action arising in tort based on the failure of a contraceptive method or sterilization procedure or on a claim that, but for the negligent conduct of another, tests or treatment would have been provided or would have been provided properly which would have made possible the prevention, cure, or amelioration of any disease, defect, deficiency, or disability; provided, however, that abortion shall not have been deemed to prevent, cure, or ameliorate any disease, defect, deficiency, or disability. The failure or refusal of any person to perform or have an abortion shall not be a defense in any action, nor shall that failure or refusal be considered in awarding damages or in imposing a penalty in any action.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.424.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/521/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 521 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4241"><b>145.4241</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4242"><b>145.4242</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4243"><b>145.4243</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4244"><b>145.4244</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4245"><b>145.4245</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4246"><b>145.4246</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4247"><b>145.4247</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4248"><b>145.4248</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.4249"><b>145.4249</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">REGULATION OF PAY TOILETS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.425">
        <h1 class="shn">145.425 PAY TOILETS IN PUBLIC PLACES; PROHIBITIONS; PENALTY.</h1>
        <p>Pay toilets and urinals in public places, public conveyances or public buildings are prohibited.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.425.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/215/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 215 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/205/#laws.2.1.0">2001 c 205 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.145.43">
        <h1>145.43 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.43.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 1990 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/153A.19#stat.153A.19.1">153A.19, subdivision 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.43.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>MS 1991 Supp [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/153A.19">153A.19, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.43.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/418/#laws.0.2.0">1984 c 418 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.43.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/182/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 182 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.43.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 1990 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/153A.19">153A.19, subd 3</a>]</p>
        </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.45"><b>145.45</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/153A.19">153A.19</a> subds 5,6]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.46"><b>145.46</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.2.45.0">1999 c 245 art 2 s 45</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.47"><b>145.47</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PRENATAL TRISOMY DIAGNOSIS<br>AWARENESS ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.471">
        <h1 class="shn">145.471 PRENATAL TRISOMY DIAGNOSIS AWARENESS ACT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.471.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Short title.</span></h2>
          <p>This section shall be known and may be cited as the "Prenatal Trisomy Diagnosis Awareness Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.471.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them:</p>
          <p>(1) "commissioner" means the commissioner of health;</p>
          <p>(2) "deliver" means providing information to an expectant parent and, if appropriate, other family members, in a written format;</p>
          <p>(3) "health care practitioner" means a medical professional that provides prenatal or postnatal care and administers or requests administration of a diagnostic or screening test to a pregnant woman that detects for trisomy conditions; and</p>
          <p>(4) "trisomy conditions" means trisomy 13, otherwise known as Patau syndrome; trisomy 18, otherwise known as Edwards syndrome; and trisomy 21, otherwise known as Down syndrome.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.471.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Health care practitioner duty.</span></h2>
          <p>A health care practitioner who orders tests for a pregnant woman to screen for trisomy conditions shall provide the information in subdivision 4 to the pregnant woman if the test reveals a positive result for any of the trisomy conditions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.471.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall make the following information available to health care practitioners:</p>
          <p>(1) up-to-date and evidence-based information about the trisomy conditions that has been reviewed by medical experts and national trisomy organizations. The information must be provided in a written or an alternative format and must include the following:</p>
          <p>(i) expected physical, developmental, educational, and psychosocial outcomes;</p>
          <p>(ii) life expectancy;</p>
          <p>(iii) the clinical course description;</p>
          <p>(iv) expected intellectual and functional development; and</p>
          <p>(v) treatment options available for the particular syndrome for which the test was positive; and</p>
          <p>(2) contact information for nonprofit organizations that provide information and support services for trisomy conditions.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall post the information in paragraph (a) on the Department of Health website.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall follow existing department practice to ensure that the information is culturally and linguistically appropriate for all recipients.</p>
          <p>(d) Any local or national organization that provides education or services related to trisomy conditions may request that the commissioner include the organization's informational material and contact information on the Department of Health website. Once a request is made, the commissioner may add the information to the website.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.471.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/28/#laws.0.1.0">2015 c 28 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SEXUAL ASSAULT VICTIMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4711">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4711 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4711.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4711">145.4711</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4713">145.4713</a>, the following definitions apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4711.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4711.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Emergency care to sexual assault victims.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emergency care to sexual assault victims" means medical examinations, procedures, and services provided at a hospital to a sexual assault victim following an alleged sexual assault.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4711.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Emergency contraception.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emergency contraception" means a drug, drug regimen, or device approved by the federal Food and Drug Administration to prevent pregnancy when administered after sexual contact, including prescription and over-the-counter hormonal emergency contraception and intrauterine devices.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4711.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Sexual assault.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sexual assault" means criminal sexual conduct in the first degree under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a>, criminal sexual conduct in the second degree under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.343">609.343</a>, criminal sexual conduct in the third degree under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.344">609.344</a>, criminal sexual conduct in the fourth degree under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a>, sexual extortion under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3458">609.3458</a>, or incest under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.365">609.365</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4711.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Sexual assault victim.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sexual assault victim" means a woman or man who alleges, or is alleged to have been, sexually assaulted and who presents at a hospital as a patient.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4711.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/42/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 42 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/11/#laws.4.31.0">1Sp2021 c 11 art 4 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4712">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4712 EMERGENCY CARE TO SEXUAL ASSAULT VICTIMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4712.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Emergency care to female sexual assault victims.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It shall be the standard of care for all hospitals and other health care providers that provide emergency care to, at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) provide each female sexual assault victim with medically and factually accurate and unbiased written and oral information about emergency contraception from the American College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists and distributed to all hospitals by the Department of Health;</p>
          <p>(2) orally inform each female sexual assault victim of the option of being provided with emergency contraception at the hospital or other health care facility; and</p>
          <p>(3) immediately provide emergency contraception to each sexual assault victim who requests it provided it is not medically contraindicated and is ordered by a legal prescriber. Emergency contraception shall be administered in accordance with current medical protocols regarding timing and dosage necessary to complete the treatment.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A hospital or health care provider may administer a pregnancy test. If the pregnancy test is positive, the hospital or health care provider does not have to comply with the provisions in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4712.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Emergency care to male and female sexual assault victims.</span></h2>
          <p>It shall be the standard of care for all hospitals and health care providers that provide emergency care to, at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) provide each sexual assault victim with factually accurate and unbiased written and oral medical information about prophylactic antibiotics for treatment of sexually transmitted infections;</p>
          <p>(2) orally inform each sexual assault victim of the option of being provided prophylactic antibiotics for treatment of sexually transmitted infections at the hospital or other health care facility; and</p>
          <p>(3) immediately provide prophylactic antibiotics for treatment of sexually transmitted infections to each sexual assault victim who requests it, provided it is not medically contraindicated and is ordered by a legal prescriber.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4712.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/42/#laws.0.2.0">2007 c 42 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.5.2.0">2023 c 52 art 5 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4713">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4713 COMPLAINTS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall accept and investigate complaints regarding hospital compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4712">145.4712</a>. The commissioner shall periodically determine whether hospitals are in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4712">145.4712</a>. Failure to comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4712">145.4712</a> may be grounds for the suspension or revocation of a hospital's license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.55#stat.144.55.6">144.55, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4713.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/42/#laws.0.3.0">2007 c 42 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4715">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4715 REPORTING PREVALENCE OF SEXUAL VIOLENCE.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of health must routinely report to the public and to the legislature data on the prevalence and incidence of sexual violence in Minnesota, to the extent federal funding is available for this purpose. The commissioner must use existing data provided by the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, or other source as identified by the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4715.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/247/#laws.2.11.0">2012 c 247 art 2 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4716">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4716 SAFE HARBOR FOR SEXUALLY EXPLOITED YOUTH.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4716.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Director.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of health shall establish a position for a director of child sex trafficking prevention.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4716.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties of director.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The director of child sex trafficking prevention is responsible for the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) developing and providing comprehensive training on sexual exploitation of youth for social service professionals, medical professionals, public health workers, and criminal justice professionals;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) collecting, organizing, maintaining, and disseminating information on sexual exploitation and services across the state, including maintaining a list of resources on the Department of Health website;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) monitoring and applying for federal funding for antitrafficking efforts that may benefit victims in the state;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) managing grant programs established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4716">145.4716</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4718">145.4718</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3241">609.3241</a>, paragraph (c), clause (3); and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.5315#stat.609.5315.5c">609.5315, subdivision 5c</a>, clause (3);</p>
          <p class="in">(5) managing the request for proposals for grants for comprehensive services, including trauma-informed, culturally specific services;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) identifying best practices in serving sexually exploited youth, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.31">260C.007, subdivision 31</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) providing oversight of and technical support to regional navigators pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4717">145.4717</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) conducting a comprehensive evaluation of the statewide program for safe harbor of sexually exploited youth; and</p>
          <p class="in">(9) developing a policy consistent with the requirements of chapter 13 for sharing data related to sexually exploited youth, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.31">260C.007, subdivision 31</a>, among regional navigators and community-based advocates.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.4716.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Youth eligible for services.</span></h2>
          <p>Youth 24 years of age or younger are eligible for all services, support, and programs provided under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4717">145.4717</a>, and all shelter, housing beds, and services provided by the commissioner of human services to sexually exploited youth and youth at risk of sexual exploitation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256K.47">256K.47</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4716.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.43.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.23.6.0">2014 c 312 art 23 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.15.1.0">2016 c 189 art 15 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.10.96.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 10 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.11.1.0">2023 c 70 art 11 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4717">
        <h1 class="shn">145.4717 REGIONAL NAVIGATOR GRANTS.</h1>
        <p class="in">The commissioner of health, through its director of child sex trafficking prevention established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.4716">145.4716</a>, shall provide grants to regional navigators serving six regions of the state to be determined by the commissioner. Each regional navigator must develop and annually submit a work plan to the director of child sex trafficking prevention. The work plans must include, but are not limited to, the following information:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) a needs statement specific to the region, including an examination of the population at risk;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) regional resources available to sexually exploited youth, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.31">260C.007, subdivision 31</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) grant goals and measurable outcomes; and</p>
        <p class="in">(4) grant activities including timelines.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.4717.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.44.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 44</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.4718">
