        <h1 class="shn">145.713 OPTOMETRIST AND PHYSICIAN PRACTICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.713.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibited conduct.</span></h2>
          <p>No optometrist or physician may:</p>
          <p>(1) condition the availability of an eye examination or the release of a prescription to a patient on a requirement that the patient agree to purchase ophthalmic goods from the optometrist or physician who performed the eye examination or from another specified optometrist or physician;</p>
          <p>(2) charge a patient a fee in addition to the optometrist's or physician's examination and fitting fees as a condition of releasing the prescription to the patient. An optometrist or physician may charge a reasonable additional fee for fitting ophthalmic goods dispensed by another practitioner if that fee is imposed at the time the fitting is performed; or</p>
          <p>(3) prescribe a manufacturer's brand name contact lens that can only be dispensed through the prescribing physician or optometrist's office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.713.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contraindications for contact lenses.</span></h2>
          <p>If an optometrist or physician determines that a patient's ocular health presents a contraindication for contact lenses, the optometrist or physician must orally inform the patient of the contraindication and must document the contraindication in the patient's records. An optometrist or physician may exclude categories of contact lenses where clinically indicated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.713.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Waivers of liability prohibited.</span></h2>
          <p>No optometrist or physician may place on a patient's prescription, require a patient to sign, or deliver to a patient a form or notice waiving liability or responsibility for the accuracy of the eye examination or the accuracy of the ophthalmic goods and ophthalmic services dispensed by another practitioner. Prohibiting waivers of liability under this subdivision does not impose liability on an optometrist or physician for the ophthalmic goods or ophthalmic services dispensed by another practitioner pursuant to the optometrist's or physician's prescription.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.713.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Provider-patient relationship required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "contact lens" means any lens that is placed directly on the surface of the eye, whether or not the lens is intended to correct a visual defect, including any cosmetic, therapeutic, or corrective lens;</p>
          <p>(2) "ophthalmic prescription" means a handwritten or electronic order of a provider that includes:</p>
          <p>(i) in the case of contact lenses, all information required by the Fairness to Contact Lens Consumers Act, United States Code, title 15, section 7601, et seq.;</p>
          <p>(ii) in the case of prescription eyeglasses, all information required by the Ophthalmic Practice Rule, also known as the Eyeglass Rule, Code of Federal Regulations, title 16, part 456; and</p>
          <p>(iii) necessary and appropriate information for the dispensing of prescription eyeglasses or contact lenses for a patient, including, at a minimum, the provider's name, the physical address of the provider's practice, and the provider's telephone number; and</p>
          <p>(3) "provider" means an optometrist or physician.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of a provider prescribing ophthalmic goods to a patient, the provider must establish a provider-patient relationship through an examination pursuant to paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(c) An examination meets the requirements of paragraph (b) if it takes place:</p>
          <p>(1) in person;</p>
          <p>(2) through face-to-face interactive, two-way, real-time communication; or</p>
          <p>(3) through store-and-forward technologies when all of the following conditions are met:</p>
          <p>(i) the provider obtains an updated medical history and makes a diagnosis at the time of prescribing;</p>
          <p>(ii) the provider conforms to the standard of care expected of in-person care as appropriate to the patient's age and presenting condition, including when the standard of care requires the use of diagnostic testing and performance of a physical examination, which may be carried out through the use of peripheral devices appropriate to the patient's condition;</p>
          <p>(iii) the ophthalmic prescription is not determined solely by use of an online questionnaire;</p>
          <p>(iv) the provider is licensed and authorized to issue an ophthalmic prescription in the state; and</p>
          <p>(v) upon request, the provider provides patient records in a timely manner in accordance with state and federal requirements.</p>
          <p>(d) This subdivision does not apply to the sale of over-the-counter eyeglasses, also known as readers, that are not designed to address the visual needs of the individual wearer.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.713.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/259/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 259 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/31/#laws.0.1.0">2019 c 31 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.7131">
        <h1 class="shn">145.7131 EXCEPTION TO EYEGLASS PRESCRIPTION EXPIRATION.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any practice to the contrary, in an emergency situation or in the case of lost glasses, an optometrist or physician may authorize a new pair of prescription eyeglasses using the prescription from the old lenses or the last prescription available.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.7131.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.10.1.0">2014 c 291 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/59/#laws.0.4.0">2017 c 59 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/170/#laws.0.4.0">2018 c 170 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.714">
        <h1 class="shn">145.714 ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <p>Failure to comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.711">145.711</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.713">145.713</a> shall be grounds for disciplinary action by the Board of Optometry or the Board of Medical Practice.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.714.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/259/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 259 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.72"><b>145.72</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/323/#laws.0.16.0">1979 c 323 s 16</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.841"><b>145.841</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/614/#laws.0.12.0">1982 c 614 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1983 c 312 art 1 s 26</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.844"><b>145.844</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/614/#laws.0.12.0">1982 c 614 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1983 c 312 art 1 s 26</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.845"><b>145.845</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/614/#laws.0.12.0">1982 c 614 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1983 c 312 art 1 s 26</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">UNIFORM DUTIES TO<br>DISABLED PERSONS ACT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.851">
        <h1 class="shn">145.851 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <p>In sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.851">145.851</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.858">145.858</a>:</p>
        <p>(1) "disabled condition" means the condition of being unconscious, semiconscious, incoherent, or otherwise incapacitated to communicate;</p>
        <p>(2) "disabled person" means a person in a disabled condition;</p>
        <p>(3) "the emergency symbol" means the caduceus inscribed within a six-barred cross used by the American Medical Association to denote emergency information;</p>
        <p>(4) "identifying device" means an identifying bracelet, necklace, metal tag, or similar device bearing the emergency symbol and the information needed in an emergency;</p>
        <p>(5) "medical practitioner" means a person licensed or authorized to practice medicine, osteopathic medicine, and the healing arts.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.851.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 428 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/119/#laws.0.7.0">2016 c 119 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.852">
        <h1 class="shn">145.852 IDENTIFYING DEVICES FOR PERSONS HAVING CERTAIN CONDITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.852.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who suffers from epilepsy, diabetes, a cardiac condition, or any other type of illness that causes temporary blackouts, semiconscious periods, or complete unconsciousness, or who suffers from a condition requiring specific medication or medical treatment, is allergic to certain medications or items used in medical treatment, wears contact lenses, or is unable to communicate coherently or effectively in the English language, is authorized and encouraged to wear an identifying device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.852.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Identification card.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person may carry an identification card bearing the person's name, type of medical condition, physician's name, and other medical information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.852.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Consent.</span></h2>
          <p>By wearing an identifying device a person gives consent for any law enforcement officer or medical practitioner who finds the person in a disabled condition to make a reasonable search of the person's clothing or other effects for an identification card of the type described in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.852.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 428 s 2</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.853">
        <h1 class="shn">145.853 DUTY OF LAW ENFORCEMENT OFFICER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.853.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Diligent effort required.</span></h2>
          <p>A law enforcement officer shall make a diligent effort to determine whether any disabled person found is a person having epilepsy or a diabetic, or suffers from some other type of illness that would cause the condition. Whenever feasible, this effort shall be made before the person is charged with a crime or taken to a place of detention.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.853.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Search.</span></h2>
          <p>In seeking to determine whether a disabled person suffers from an illness, a law enforcement officer shall make a reasonable search for an identifying device and an identification card of the type described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.852#stat.145.852.2">145.852, subdivision 2</a>, and examine them for emergency information. The law enforcement officer may not search for an identifying device or an identification card in a manner or to an extent that would appear to a reasonable person in the circumstances to cause an unreasonable risk of worsening the disabled person's condition. The law enforcement officer may not remove an identifying device or an identification card from the possession of a disabled person unless the removal is necessary for law enforcement purposes or to protect the safety of the disabled person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.853.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duty; absence of device.</span></h2>
          <p>A law enforcement officer who finds a disabled person without an identifying device or identification card is not relieved of the duty to that person to make a diligent effort to ascertain the existence of any illness causing the disabled condition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.853.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Actions.</span></h2>
          <p>A cause of action against a law enforcement officer does not arise from the officer's making a reasonable search of the disabled person to locate an identifying device or identification card, even though the person is not wearing an identifying device or carrying an identification card.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.853.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Notification; medical care.</span></h2>
          <p>A law enforcement officer who determines or has reason to believe that a disabled person is suffering from an illness causing the person's condition shall promptly notify the person's physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant, if practicable. If the officer is unable to ascertain the physician's, advanced practice registered nurse's, or physician assistant's identity or to communicate with the physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant, the officer shall make a reasonable effort to cause the disabled person to be transported immediately to a medical practitioner or to a facility where medical treatment is available. If the officer believes it unduly dangerous to move the disabled person, the officer shall make a reasonable effort to obtain the assistance of a medical practitioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.853.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 428 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/10/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.44.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.64.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.75.0">2022 c 58 s 75</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.854">
        <h1 class="shn">145.854 DUTY OF MEDICAL PRACTITIONERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.854.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Search.</span></h2>
          <p>A medical practitioner, in discharging a duty to a disabled person whom the practitioner has undertaken to examine or treat, shall make a reasonable search for an identifying device or identification card of the type described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.852#stat.145.852.2">145.852, subdivision 2</a> and examine them for emergency information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.854.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Actions.</span></h2>
          <p>A cause of action against a medical practitioner does not arise from the practitioner's making a reasonable search of a disabled person to locate an identifying device or identification card, even though the person is not wearing an identifying device or carrying an identification card.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.854.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.4.0">1973 c 428 s 4</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.855">
        <h1 class="shn">145.855 DUTY OF OTHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.855.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notification; search.</span></h2>
          <p>A person, other than a law enforcement officer or medical practitioner, who finds a disabled person shall make a reasonable effort to notify a law enforcement officer. If a law enforcement officer or medical practitioner is not present, a person who finds a disabled person may (1) make a reasonable search for an identifying device, and (2) if the identifying device is found may make a reasonable search for an identification card of the type described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.852#stat.145.852.2">145.852, subdivision 2</a>. If a device or card is located, the person making the search shall attempt promptly to bring its contents to the attention of a law enforcement officer or medical practitioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.855.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Actions.</span></h2>
          <p>A cause of action does not arise from a reasonable search to locate an identifying device or identification card as authorized by subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.855.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.5.0">1973 c 428 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.856">
        <h1 class="shn">145.856 FALSIFYING IDENTIFICATION OR MISREPRESENTING CONDITION; PENALTY.</h1>
        <p>A person who with intent to deceive provides, wears, uses, or possesses a false identifying device or identification card of the type described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.852#stat.145.852.2">145.852, subdivision 2</a> is guilty of a misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.856.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.6.0">1973 c 428 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.857">
        <h1 class="shn">145.857 OTHER DUTIES.</h1>
        <p>The duties imposed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.851">145.851</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.858">145.858</a> are in addition to, and not in limitation of, other duties existing under the law of this state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.857.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/428/#laws.0.7.0">1973 c 428 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.858">
        <h1 class="shn">145.858 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.851">145.851</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.858">145.858</a> may be cited as the "Uniform Duties to Disabled Persons Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.858.history
			">

      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.863"><b>145.863</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.209.0">1976 c 222 s 209</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.864"><b>145.864</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.209.0">1976 c 222 s 209</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.145.865">
        <h1>145.865 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.865.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.209.0">1976 c 222 s 209</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.865.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/315/#laws.0.26.0">1975 c 315 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.865.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.209.0">1976 c 222 s 209</a>]</p>
