      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.866"><b>145.866</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.209.0">1976 c 222 s 209</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SPECIAL DIETS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.867">
        <h1 class="shn">145.867 PERSONS REQUIRING SPECIAL DIETS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.867.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Public facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Public facility" means an auditorium, concert hall, sports stadium, sports arena, or theater.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.867.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Identification card for individuals needing a special diet.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health shall make special diet identification cards available to physicians, advanced practice registered nurses, physician assistants, and persons with diabetes and other conditions requiring special diets. The identification card must contain spaces for: (1) the person's name, address, and signature; (2) the physician's, advanced practice registered nurse's, or physician assistant's name, telephone number, and signature; (3) a description of the person's medical condition; and (4) an expiration date. The card must also contain the following provision, in identical or substantially similar language: "The owner of this card is exempted by the commissioner of health from prohibitions on bringing outside food and drink into a public facility." Persons with medical conditions requiring a special diet may ask their physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant to fill out and sign the card. The physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant shall fill out and sign the card if, in the physician's, advanced practice registered nurse's, or physician assistant's medical judgment, the person has a medical condition that requires a special diet. Persons with diabetes shall be automatically assumed by physicians, advanced practice registered nurses, and physician assistants to require special diets. Special diet identification cards shall be valid for five years. Persons with a medical condition requiring a special diet may request a new card from their physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant up to six months before the expiration date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.867.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exemption from food and drink prohibitions.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons with medical conditions requiring a special diet who present a valid special diet identification card to any employee of a public facility shall be allowed to bring in outside food and drink, subject to the limitations in subdivision 4. To be valid, the card must be filled out according to subdivision 2 and must be current. Persons with special diet identification cards must obey all other food and drink regulations established by a public facility including prohibitions on eating or drinking in certain areas of the public facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.867.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Limitation on exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Public facilities may limit the amount of food and drink that may be brought into a public facility by a person with a special diet identification card to the amount that can reasonably be consumed by a single individual. Public facilities may also place limits on the size of any food or drink container carried in, if the container would be a safety hazard or interfere with other patrons or customers. Public facilities may also require persons displaying a special diet identification card to show some other form of identification.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.867.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.32.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/59/#laws.0.5.0">2017 c 59 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.87">
        <h1 class="shn">145.87 HOME VISITING FOR PREGNANT WOMEN AND FAMILIES WITH YOUNG CHILDREN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.87.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The terms defined in this subdivision apply to this section and have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Evidence-based home visiting program" means a program that:</p>
          <p>(1) is based on a clear, consistent program or model that is research-based and grounded in relevant, empirically based knowledge;</p>
          <p>(2) is linked to program-determined outcomes and is associated with a national organization, institution of higher education, or national or state public health institute;</p>
          <p>(3) has comprehensive home visitation standards that ensure high-quality service delivery and continuous quality improvement;</p>
          <p>(4) has demonstrated significant, sustained positive outcomes; and</p>
          <p>(5) either:</p>
          <p>(i) has been evaluated using rigorous randomized controlled research designs and the evaluation results have been published in a peer-reviewed journal; or</p>
          <p>(ii) is based on quasi-experimental research using two or more separate, comparable client samples.</p>
          <p>(c) "Evidence-informed home visiting program" means a program that:</p>
          <p>(1) has data or evidence demonstrating effectiveness at achieving positive outcomes for pregnant women or young children; and</p>
          <p>(2) either:</p>
          <p>(i) has an active evaluation of the program; or</p>
          <p>(ii) has a plan and timeline for an active evaluation of the program to be conducted.</p>
          <p>(d) "Health equity" means every individual has a fair opportunity to attain the individual's full health potential and no individual is disadvantaged from achieving this potential.</p>
          <p>(e) "Promising practice home visiting program" means a program that has shown improvement toward achieving positive outcomes for pregnant women or young children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.87.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants for home visiting programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health shall award grants to community health boards, nonprofit organizations, and Tribal nations to start up, sustain, or expand voluntary home visiting programs serving pregnant women or families with young children. Home visiting programs supported under this section shall provide voluntary home visits by early childhood professionals or health professionals, including but not limited to nurses, social workers, early childhood educators, and trained paraprofessionals. Grant money shall be used to:</p>
          <p>(1) establish, sustain, or expand evidence-based, evidence-informed, or promising practice home visiting programs that address health equity and utilize community-driven health strategies;</p>
          <p>(2) serve families with young children or pregnant women who have high needs or are high-risk, including but not limited to a family with low income, a parent or pregnant woman with a mental illness or a substance use disorder, or a parent or pregnant woman experiencing housing instability or domestic abuse; and</p>
          <p>(3) improve program outcomes in two or more of the following areas:</p>
          <p>(i) maternal and newborn health;</p>
          <p>(ii) school readiness and achievement;</p>
          <p>(iii) family economic self-sufficiency;</p>
          <p>(iv) coordination and referral for other community resources and supports;</p>
          <p>(v) reduction in child injuries, abuse, or neglect; or</p>
          <p>(vi) reduction in crime or domestic violence.</p>
          <p>(b) Grants awarded to evidence-informed and promising practice home visiting programs must include money to evaluate program outcomes for up to four of the areas listed in paragraph (a), clause (3).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.87.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant prioritization.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In awarding grants, the commissioner shall give priority to community health boards, nonprofit organizations, and Tribal nations seeking to expand home visiting services with community or regional partnerships.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall allocate at least 75 percent of the grant money awarded each grant cycle to evidence-based home visiting programs that address health equity and up to 25 percent of the grant money awarded each grant cycle to evidence-informed or promising practice home visiting programs that address health equity and utilize community-driven health strategies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.87.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may provide training and technical assistance and administer and evaluate the program. The commissioner may contract for training, capacity-building support for grantees or potential grantees, technical assistance, and evaluation support.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.87.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Use of state general fund appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>Appropriations dedicated to establishing, sustaining, or expanding evidence-based home visiting programs shall, for grants awarded on or after July 1, 2021, be awarded according to this section. This section shall not govern grant awards of federal funds for home visiting programs and shall not govern grant awards using state general fund appropriations dedicated to establishing or expanding nurse-family partnership home visiting programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.87.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.3.21.0">2021 c 30 art 3 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.58.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 58</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MATERNAL AND CHILD HEALTH</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.88">
        <h1 class="shn">145.88 PURPOSE.</h1>
        <p>Federal money received by the Minnesota Department of Health, pursuant to United States Code, title 42, sections 701 to 709, shall be expended to:</p>
        <p>(1) assure access to quality maternal and child health services for mothers and children, especially those of low income and with limited availability to health services and those children at risk of physical, neurological, emotional, and developmental problems arising from chemical abuse by a mother during pregnancy;</p>
        <p>(2) reduce infant mortality and the incidence of preventable diseases and disabling conditions among children;</p>
        <p>(3) reduce the need for inpatient and long-term care services and to otherwise promote the health of mothers and children, especially by providing preventive and primary care services for low-income mothers and children and prenatal, delivery and postpartum care for low-income mothers;</p>
        <p>(4) provide rehabilitative services for blind and disabled children under age 16 receiving benefits under title XVI of the Social Security Act; and</p>
        <p>(5) provide and locate medical, surgical, corrective and other service for children with special health care needs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.88.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/431/#laws.0.1.0">1982 c 431 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/542/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 542 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.2.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/62/#laws.0.5.0">2013 c 62 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.881"><b>145.881 </b>MS 2010 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.7.45.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 7 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/85/#laws.0.4.0">2007 c 85 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.8811">
        <h1 class="shn">145.8811 MATERNAL AND CHILD HEALTH ADVISORY TASK FORCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.8811.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Composition of task force.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish and appoint a Maternal and Child Health Advisory Task Force consisting of 15 members who will provide equal representation from:</p>
          <p>(1) professionals with expertise in maternal and child health services;</p>
          <p>(2) representatives of community health boards as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.02">145A.02</a>, subdivision 5; and</p>
          <p>(3) consumer representatives interested in the health of mothers and children.</p>
          <p>No members shall be employees of the Minnesota Department of Health. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a> governs the Maternal and Child Health Advisory Task Force. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>, the Maternal and Child Health Advisory Task Force does not expire.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.8811.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory task force shall meet on a regular basis to perform the following duties:</p>
          <p>(1) review and report on the health care needs of mothers and children throughout the state of Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) review and report on the type, frequency, and impact of maternal and child health care services provided to mothers and children under existing maternal and child health care programs, including programs administered by the commissioner of health;</p>
          <p>(3) establish, review, and report to the commissioner a list of program guidelines and criteria which the advisory task force considers essential to providing an effective maternal and child health care program to low-income populations and high-risk persons and fulfilling the purposes defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.88">145.88</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) make recommendations to the commissioner for the use of other federal and state funds available to meet maternal and child health needs;</p>
          <p>(5) make recommendations to the commissioner of health on priorities for funding the following maternal and child health services:</p>
          <p>(i) prenatal, delivery, and postpartum care;</p>
          <p>(ii) comprehensive health care for children, especially from birth through five years of age;</p>
          <p>(iii) adolescent health services;</p>
          <p>(iv) family planning services;</p>
          <p>(v) preventive dental care;</p>
          <p>(vi) special services for chronically ill and disabled children; and</p>
          <p>(vii) any other services that promote the health of mothers and children; and</p>
          <p>(6) establish in consultation with the commissioner statewide outcomes that will improve the health status of mothers and children.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.8811.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/247/#laws.2.7.0">2012 c 247 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.7.29.0">2014 c 291 art 7 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/42/#laws.0.3.0">2015 c 42 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.882">
        <h1 class="shn">145.882 MATERNAL AND CHILD HEALTH BLOCK GRANT DISTRIBUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>Any decrease in the amount of federal funding to the state for the maternal and child health block grant must be apportioned to reflect a proportional decrease for each recipient. Any increase in the amount of federal funding to the state must be distributed under subdivisions 2 and 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Allocation to commissioner of health.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning January 1, 1986, up to one-third of the total maternal and child health block grant money may be retained by the commissioner of health to:</p>
          <p>(1) meet federal maternal and child block grant requirements of a statewide needs assessment every five years and prepare the annual federal block grant application and report;</p>
          <p>(2) collect and disseminate statewide data on the health status of mothers and children within one year of the end of the year;</p>
          <p>(3) provide technical assistance to community health boards in meeting statewide outcomes;</p>
          <p>(4) evaluate the impact of maternal and child health activities on the health status of mothers and children;</p>
          <p>(5) provide services to children under age 16 receiving benefits under title XVI of the Social Security Act; and</p>
          <p>(6) perform other maternal and child health activities listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.88">145.88</a> and as deemed necessary by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allocation to community health boards.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The maternal and child health block grant money remaining after distributions made under subdivision 2 must be allocated according to the formula in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.131#stat.145A.131.2">145A.131, subdivision 2</a>, for distribution to community health boards.</p>
          <p>(b) A community health board that receives funding under this section shall provide at least a 50 percent match for funds received under United States Code, title 42, sections 701 to 709. Eligible funds must be used to meet match requirements. Eligible funds include funds from local property taxes, reimbursements from third parties, fees, other funds, donations, nonfederal grants, or state funds received under the local public health grant defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.131">145A.131</a>, that are used for maternal and child health activities as described in subdivision 7.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Nonparticipating community health boards.</span></h2>
          <p>If a community health board decides not to participate in maternal and child health block grant activities under subdivision 3 or the commissioner determines under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.131#stat.145A.131.7">145A.131, subdivision 7</a>, not to fund the community health board, the commissioner is responsible for directing maternal and child health block grant activities in that community health board's geographic area. The commissioner may elect to directly provide public health activities to meet the statewide outcomes or to contract with other governmental units or nonprofit organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Use of block grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>Maternal and child health block grant money allocated to a community health board under this section must be used for qualified programs for high risk and low-income individuals. Block grant money must be used for programs that:</p>
          <p>(1) specifically address the highest risk populations, particularly low-income and minority groups with a high rate of infant mortality and children with low birth weight, by providing services, including prepregnancy family planning services, calculated to produce measurable decreases in infant mortality rates, instances of children with low birth weight, and medical complications associated with pregnancy and childbirth, including infant mortality, low birth rates, and medical complications arising from chemical abuse by a mother during pregnancy;</p>
          <p>(2) specifically target pregnant women whose age, medical condition, maternal history, or chemical abuse substantially increases the likelihood of complications associated with pregnancy and childbirth or the birth of a child with an illness, disability, or special medical needs;</p>
          <p>(3) specifically address the health needs of young children who have or are likely to have a chronic disease or disability or special medical needs, including physical, neurological, emotional, and developmental problems that arise from chemical abuse by a mother during pregnancy;</p>
          <p>(4) provide family planning and preventive medical care for specifically identified target populations, such as minority and low-income teenagers, in a manner calculated to decrease the occurrence of inappropriate pregnancy and minimize the risk of complications associated with pregnancy and childbirth;</p>
          <p>(5) specifically address the frequency and severity of childhood and adolescent health issues, including injuries in high risk target populations by providing services calculated to produce measurable decreases in mortality and morbidity;</p>
          <p>(6) specifically address preventing child abuse and neglect, reducing juvenile delinquency, promoting positive parenting and resiliency in children, and promoting family health and economic sufficiency through public health nurse home visits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.17">145A.17</a>; or</p>
          <p>(7) specifically address nutritional issues of women, infants, and young children through WIC clinic services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.882.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.882.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/431/#laws.0.3.0">1982 c 431 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/312/#laws.4.2.0">1983 c 312 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.19.18.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 19 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/209/#laws.0.33.0">1987 c 209 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.24.0">1987 c 309 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.33.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 33</a>-35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/542/#laws.0.4.0">1990 c 542 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.4.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 4</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.7.29.0">2014 c 291 art 7 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.8821">
        <h1 class="shn">145.8821 ACCOUNTABILITY.</h1>
        <p>(a) Coordinating with accountability measures outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.131#stat.145A.131.7">145A.131, subdivision 7</a>, each community health board that receives money under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.882#stat.145.882.3">145.882, subdivision 3</a>, shall select by February 1, 2005, and every five years thereafter, up to two statewide maternal and child health outcomes.</p>
        <p>(b) For the period January 1, 2004, to December 31, 2005, each community health board must work toward the Healthy People 2010 goal to reduce the state's percentage of low birth weight infants.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner shall monitor and evaluate whether each community health board has made sufficient progress toward the selected outcomes established in paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(d) Community health boards shall provide the commissioner with annual information necessary to evaluate progress toward selected statewide outcomes and to meet federal reporting requirements.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.8821.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.9.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.27.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.7.29.0">2014 c 291 art 7 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.883">
        <h1 class="shn">145.883 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.882">145.882</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.883">145.883</a>, the terms defined in this section shall have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Qualified program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Qualified program" means a program with professional maternal and child health care staff which is established for the purpose of providing one or more essential services in maternal and child health care to target populations of low income and high risk persons. Nothing in this subdivision shall imply that every person served must take a means test.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Low income.</span></h2>
          <p>"Low income" means an individual or family income determined to be at or below 175 percent of the official poverty line established by the Office of Management and Budget and revised annually in accordance with United States Code, title 42, section 9902, as amended. With respect to an individual who is a high risk person, "low income" means that the income of the high risk person or the person's family is determined to be at or below 200 percent of the official poverty line established by the Office of Management and Budget and revised annually in accordance with United States Code, title 42, section 9902, as amended, or determined to meet the income eligibility requirements of medical assistance, MinnesotaCare, or the special supplemental food program for women, infants and children (WIC). The commissioner shall establish the low income level for eligibility for services to children with disabilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">High risk person.</span></h2>
          <p>"High risk person" means a mother or child with a condition which significantly increases the probability of disease, injury, death, or other adverse health-related problem. Determination that a condition results in high risk shall be based on well-validated, scientific studies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Maternal and child health block grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>"Maternal and child health block grant money" means the money received by the state from the federal maternal and child health block grant. The commissioner shall carry forward from state fiscal year 1985, and succeeding years, only sufficient money for qualified programs approved through the federal award period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.883.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Community health board.</span></h2>
          <p>"Community health board" means a community health board established, operating, and eligible for a local public health grant under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.11">145A.11</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.131">145A.131</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.883.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/312/#laws.4.3.0">1983 c 312 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.19.19.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 19 s 19</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.24.0">1987 c 309 s 24</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/36/#laws.0.3.0">1991 c 36 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.10.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/187/#laws.1.23.0">2012 c 187 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/219/#laws.7.29.0">2014 c 219 art 7 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.109.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.145.884">
        <h1>145.884 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.884.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.884.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.19.38.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 19 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.885"><b>145.885</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.886"><b>145.886</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.888"><b>145.888</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.889"><b>145.889</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.890"><b>145.890</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.8.32.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 8 s 32</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.891">
        <h1 class="shn">145.891 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a> shall be known as the "Maternal and Child Nutrition Act of 1975."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.891.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.1.0">1975 c 346 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.892">
        <h1 class="shn">145.892 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local health agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local health agency" means the community health services agency or any public or private nonprofit organization which enters into a contract with the commissioner of health pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Pregnant woman.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pregnant woman" means an individual determined by a licensed physician, advanced practice registered nurse, physician assistant, midwife, or appropriately trained registered nurse to have one or more fetuses in utero.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Lactating woman.</span></h2>
          <p>"Lactating woman" means any breast feeding individual who presents competent evidence of having been delivered of a surviving child within the 12 months immediately preceding the filing of an application for nutritional supplements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Infant.</span></h2>
          <p>"Infant" means an individual under one year of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Child.</span></h2>
          <p>"Child" means an individual one to five years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Nutritional risk.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nutritional risk" means individuals with any of the following characteristics:</p>
          <p>(a) For pregnant and lactating women:</p>
          <p>(i) Known inadequate nutritional patterns;</p>
          <p>(ii) Anemia;</p>
          <p>(iii) History of prematurity or miscarriage; or</p>
          <p>(iv) Inadequate patterns of growth (underweight, obesity, or stunting).</p>
          <p>(b) For infants and children:</p>
          <p>(i) Low birth weight;</p>
          <p>(ii) Deficient patterns of growth;</p>
          <p>(iii) Anemia; or</p>
          <p>(iv) Known inadequate nutritional patterns.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Low birth weight.</span></h2>
          <p>"Low birth weight" means a birth weight of less than 2,500 grams.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Nutritional supplements.</span></h2>
          <p>"Nutritional supplements" means any food authorized by the commissioner to be made available under this program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.892.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of health or a representative.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.892.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.2.0">1975 c 346 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/762/#laws.0.4.0">1978 c 762 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/404/#laws.0.7.0">1986 c 404 s 7</a>; 1986 c 444; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.65.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.76.0">2022 c 58 s 76</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.893">
        <h1 class="shn">145.893 NUTRITIONAL SUPPLEMENT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.893.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Food benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible individual shall receive food benefits for the purchase of specified nutritional supplements in type and quantity approved by the commissioner. Alternate forms of delivery may be developed by the commissioner in appropriate cases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.893.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual shall be eligible for nutritional supplements who is not receiving a similar supplement under any federal, state, or local program and</p>
          <p>(1) is pregnant or lactating; or</p>
          <p>(2) is an infant or a child; and</p>
          <p>(3) is eligible for or a recipient of any form of public assistance authorized by law and is certified by the local health agency to be a nutritional risk; or</p>
          <p>(4) is certified by the local health agency to be a nutritional risk and is without sufficient resources to purchase necessary nutritional supplements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.893.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cessation.</span></h2>
          <p>Eligibility for nutritional supplements shall cease upon certification by the local health agency that the individual is no longer a nutritional risk, but in no case later than:</p>
          <p>(1) for lactating women, 12 months after the birth of a surviving child; and</p>
          <p>(2) for children, at five years of age.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.893.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.3.0">1975 c 346 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/762/#laws.0.5.0">1978 c 762 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.3.22.0">2021 c 30 art 3 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.894">
        <h1 class="shn">145.894 STATE COMMISSIONER OF HEALTH; DUTIES, RESPONSIBILITIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of health shall:</p>
        <p>(1) develop a comprehensive state plan for the delivery of nutritional supplements to pregnant and lactating women, infants, and children;</p>
        <p>(2) contract with existing local public or private nonprofit organizations for the administration of the nutritional supplement program;</p>
        <p>(3) develop and implement a public education program promoting the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a>, and provide for the delivery of individual and family nutrition education and counseling at project sites. The education programs must include a campaign to promote breast feeding;</p>
        <p>(4) develop in cooperation with other agencies and vendors a uniform state food benefit system for the delivery of nutritional supplements;</p>
        <p>(5) authorize local health agencies to issue food benefits trimonthly to some or all eligible individuals served by the agency, provided the agency demonstrates that the federal minimum requirements for providing nutrition education will continue to be met and that the quality of nutrition education and health services provided by the agency will not be adversely impacted;</p>
        <p>(6) investigate and implement a system to reduce the cost of nutritional supplements and maintain ongoing negotiations with nonparticipating manufacturers and suppliers to maximize cost savings;</p>
        <p>(7) develop, analyze, and evaluate the health aspects of the nutritional supplement program and establish nutritional guidelines for the program;</p>
        <p>(8) apply for, administer, and annually expend at least 99 percent of available federal or private funds;</p>
        <p>(9) aggressively market services to eligible individuals by conducting ongoing outreach activities and by coordinating with and providing marketing materials and technical assistance to local human services and community service agencies and nonprofit service providers;</p>
        <p>(10) determine, on July 1 of each year, the number of pregnant women participating in each special supplemental food program for women, infants, and children (WIC);</p>
        <p>(11) promulgate all rules necessary to carry out the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a>; and</p>
        <p>(12) ensure that any state appropriation to supplement the federal program is spent consistent with federal requirements.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.894.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.4.0">1975 c 346 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/404/#laws.0.8.0">1986 c 404 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.45.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.1.17.0">1989 c 282 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.3.5.0">1990 c 568 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.2.20.0">1997 c 7 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.3.23.0">2021 c 30 art 3 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.895">
        <h1 class="shn">145.895 DEPARTMENT OF HUMAN SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services shall cooperate with the commissioner of health in identifying eligible individuals. The commissioner of human services shall provide a procedure for the notification of pregnant or lactating women, infants and children receiving any form of public assistance of eligibility for benefits under this program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.895.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.5.0">1975 c 346 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.896">
        <h1 class="shn">145.896 PROGRAM NOT A SUBSTITUTE OR REPLACEMENT.</h1>
        <p>This program shall not be a replacement or substitute for any other local, state, or federal program administered through the Departments of Health or Human Services, nor shall the value of the nutritional supplements be included in eligibility determination for other assistance programs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.896.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 346 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.5.58.0">1984 c 654 art 5 s 58</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.897">
        <h1 class="shn">145.897 FOOD BENEFITS.</h1>
        <p>Food benefits issued pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.891">145.891</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.897">145.897</a> shall be only for the purchase of those foods determined by the United States Department of Agriculture to be desirable nutritional supplements for pregnant and lactating women, infants and children.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.897.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/346/#laws.0.7.0">1975 c 346 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.45.0">1977 c 305 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.3.24.0">2021 c 30 art 3 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.898">
        <h1 class="shn">145.898 SUDDEN INFANT DEATH.</h1>
        <p>The Department of Health shall develop uniform investigative guidelines and protocols for coroners and medical examiners conducting death investigations and autopsies of children under two years of age.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.898.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.36.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 36</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.899">
        <h1 class="shn">145.899 WIC FOOD BENEFITS FOR ORGANICS.</h1>
        <p>Food benefits for the special supplemental nutrition program for women, infants, and children (WIC) may be used to purchase cost-neutral organic WIC allowable food. The commissioner of health shall regularly evaluate the list of WIC allowable food in accordance with federal requirements and shall add to the list any organic WIC allowable foods determined to be cost-neutral.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.899.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/114/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 114 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.3.25.0">2021 c 30 art 3 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.90"><b>145.90</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/211/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 211 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.901">
        <h1 class="shn">145.901 MATERNAL DEATH STUDIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.901.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health may conduct maternal death studies to assist the planning, implementation, and evaluation of medical, health, and welfare service systems and to reduce the numbers of preventable maternal deaths in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.901.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Access to data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health has access to medical data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.384#stat.13.384.1">13.384, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), medical examiner data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.83#stat.13.83.1">13.83, subdivision 1</a>, and health records created, maintained, or stored by providers as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.291#stat.144.291.2">144.291, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c), without the consent of the subject of the data, and without the consent of the parent, spouse, other guardian, or legal representative of the subject of the data, when the subject of the data is a woman who died during a pregnancy or within 12 months of a fetal death, a live birth, or other termination of a pregnancy.</p>
          <p>The commissioner has access only to medical data and health records related to deaths that occur on or after July 1, 2000, including the names of the providers, clinics, or other health services such as family home visiting programs; the women, infants, and children (WIC) program; prescription monitoring programs; and behavioral health services, where care was received before, during, or related to the pregnancy or death. The commissioner has access to records maintained by a medical examiner, a coroner, or hospitals or to hospital discharge data, for the purpose of providing the name and location of any pre-pregnancy, prenatal, or other care received by the subject of the data up to one year after the end of the pregnancy.</p>
          <p>(b) The provider or responsible authority that creates, maintains, or stores the data shall furnish the data upon the request of the commissioner. The provider or responsible authority may charge a fee for providing the data, not to exceed the actual cost of retrieving and duplicating the data.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall make a good faith reasonable effort to notify the parent, spouse, other guardian, or legal representative of the subject of the data before collecting data on the subject. For purposes of this paragraph, "reasonable effort" means one notice is sent by certified mail to the last known address of the parent, spouse, guardian, or legal representative informing the recipient of the data collection and offering a public health nurse support visit if desired.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner does not have access to coroner or medical examiner data that are part of an active investigation as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.83">13.83</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner may request and receive from a coroner or medical examiner the name of the health care provider that provided prenatal, postpartum, or other health services to the subject of the data.</p>
          <p>(f) The commissioner may access Department of Human Services data to identify sources of care and services to assist with the evaluation of welfare systems, including housing, to reduce preventable maternal deaths.</p>
          <p>(g) The commissioner may request and receive law enforcement reports or incident reports related to the subject of the data.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.901.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Management of records.</span></h2>
          <p>After the commissioner has collected all data about a subject of a maternal death study needed to perform the study, the data from source records obtained under subdivision 2, other than data identifying the subject, must be transferred to separate records to be maintained by the commissioner. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/138.17">138.17</a>, after the data have been transferred, all source records obtained under subdivision 2 possessed by the commissioner must be destroyed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.901.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Classification of data.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Data provided to the commissioner from source records under subdivision 2, including identifying information on individual providers, data subjects, or their children, and data derived by the commissioner under subdivision 3 for the purpose of carrying out maternal death studies, are classified as confidential data on individuals or confidential data on decedents, as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.3">13.02, subdivision 3</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.10#stat.13.10.1">13.10, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(b) Information classified under paragraph (a) shall not be subject to discovery or introduction into evidence in any administrative, civil, or criminal proceeding. Such information otherwise available from an original source shall not be immune from discovery or barred from introduction into evidence merely because it was utilized by the commissioner in carrying out maternal death studies.</p>
          <p>(c) Summary data on maternal death studies created by the commissioner, which does not identify individual data subjects or individual providers, shall be public in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.05#stat.13.05.7">13.05, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Data provided by the commissioner of human services to the commissioner of health under this section retain the same classification the data held when retained by the commissioner of human services, as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.03#stat.13.03.4">13.03, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.901.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Maternal Mortality Review Committee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health shall convene a Maternal Mortality Review Committee to conduct maternal death study reviews, make recommendations, and publicly share summary information. The commissioner shall appoint members to the review committee, and membership may include but is not limited to medical examiners or coroners, representatives of health care institutions that provide care to pregnant women, obstetric and midwifery practitioners, Medicaid representatives, representatives of state agencies, individuals from communities with disparate rates of maternal mortality, and other subject matter experts as appropriate. Committee membership shall not exceed 25 members. The review committee shall review data from source records obtained under subdivision 2, other than data identifying the subject or the provider.</p>
          <p>(b) A person attending a Maternal Mortality Review Committee meeting shall not disclose what transpired at the meeting, except as necessary to carry out the purposes of the review committee. The proceedings and records of the review committee are protected nonpublic data as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.13">13.02, subdivision 13</a>. Discovery and introduction into evidence in legal proceedings of case review committee proceedings and records, and testimony in legal proceedings by review committee members and persons presenting information to the review committee, shall occur in compliance with the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.7">142A.03, subdivision 7</a>, paragraph (e).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.901.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/211/#laws.0.3.0">2001 c 211 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.10.15.0">2007 c 147 art 10 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.2.4.0">2020 c 83 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.3.37.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 3 s 37</a>-39; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.902">
        <h1 class="shn">145.902 GIVE LIFE A CHANCE; SAFE PLACE FOR NEWBORNS DUTIES; IMMUNITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.902.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, a "safe place" means a hospital licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.50">144.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.56">144.56</a>, a health care provider who provides urgent care medical services, or an ambulance service licensed under chapter 144E dispatched in response to a 911 call from a mother or a person with the mother's permission to relinquish a newborn infant.</p>
          <p>(b) A safe place shall receive a newborn left with an employee on the premises of the safe place during its hours of operation, provided that:</p>
          <p>(1) the newborn was born within seven days of being left at the safe place, as determined within a reasonable degree of medical certainty; and</p>
          <p>(2) the newborn is left in an unharmed condition.</p>
          <p>(c) The safe place must not inquire as to the identity of the mother or the person leaving the newborn or call the police, provided the newborn is unharmed when presented to the hospital. The safe place may ask the mother or the person leaving the newborn about the medical history of the mother or newborn but the mother or the person leaving the newborn is not required to provide any information. The safe place may provide the mother or the person leaving the newborn with information about how to contact relevant social service agencies.</p>
          <p>(d) A safe place that is a health care provider who provides urgent care medical services shall dial 911, advise the dispatcher that the call is being made from a safe place for newborns, and ask the dispatcher to send an ambulance or take other appropriate action to transport the newborn to a hospital. An ambulance with whom a newborn is left shall transport the newborn to a hospital for care. Hospitals must receive a newborn left with a safe place and make the report as required in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.902.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 24 hours of receiving a newborn under this section, the hospital must inform the responsible social service agency that a newborn has been left at the hospital, but must not do so in the presence of the mother or the person leaving the newborn. The hospital must provide necessary care to the newborn pending assumption of legal responsibility by the responsible social service agency pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.139#stat.260C.139.5">260C.139, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.902.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Immunity.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A safe place with responsibility for performing duties under this section, and any employee, doctor, ambulance personnel, or other medical professional working at the safe place, are immune from any criminal liability that otherwise might result from their actions, if they are acting in good faith in receiving a newborn, and are immune from any civil liability that otherwise might result from merely receiving a newborn.</p>
          <p>(b) A safe place performing duties under this section, or an employee, doctor, ambulance personnel, or other medical professional working at the safe place who is a mandated reporter under chapter 260E, is immune from any criminal or civil liability that otherwise might result from the failure to make a report under that section if the person is acting in good faith in complying with this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.902.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/421/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 421 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.2.1.0">2012 c 216 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.29.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.903">
        <h1 class="shn">145.903 SCHOOL-BASED HEALTH CENTERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.903.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "School-based health center" or "comprehensive school-based health center" means a safety net health care delivery model that is located in or near a school facility and that offers comprehensive health care, including preventive and behavioral health services, provided by licensed and qualified health professionals in accordance with federal, state, and local law. When not located on school property, the school-based health center must have an established relationship with one or more schools in the community and operate to primarily serve those student groups.</p>
          <p>(c) "Sponsoring organization" means any of the following that operate a school-based health center:</p>
          <p>(1) health care providers;</p>
          <p>(2) community clinics;</p>
          <p>(3) hospitals;</p>
          <p>(4) federally qualified health centers and look-alikes as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.9269">145.9269</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) health care foundations or nonprofit organizations;</p>
          <p>(6) higher education institutions; or</p>
          <p>(7) local health departments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.903.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Expansion of Minnesota school-based health centers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health shall administer a program to provide grants to school districts and school-based health centers to support existing centers and facilitate the growth of school-based health centers in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) Grant funds distributed under this subdivision shall be used to support new or existing school-based health centers that:</p>
          <p>(1) operate in partnership with a school or school district and with the permission of the school or school district board;</p>
          <p>(2) provide health services through a sponsoring organization that meets the requirements in subdivision 1, paragraph (c); and</p>
          <p>(3) provide health services to all students and youth within a school or school district, regardless of ability to pay, insurance coverage, or immigration status, and in accordance with federal, state, and local law.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of health shall administer a grant to a nonprofit organization to facilitate a community of practice among school-based health centers to improve quality, equity, and sustainability of care delivered through school-based health centers; encourage cross-sharing among school-based health centers; support existing clinics; and expand school-based health centers in new communities in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(d) Grant recipients shall report their activities and annual performance measures as defined by the commissioner in a format and time specified by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioners of health and of education shall coordinate the projects and initiatives funded under this section with other efforts at the local, state, or national level to avoid duplication and promote coordinated efforts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.903.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">School-based health center services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Services provided by a school-based health center may include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) preventive health care;</p>
          <p>(2) chronic medical condition management, including diabetes and asthma care;</p>
          <p>(3) mental health care and crisis management;</p>
          <p>(4) acute care for illness and injury;</p>
          <p>(5) oral health care;</p>
          <p>(6) vision care;</p>
          <p>(7) nutritional counseling;</p>
          <p>(8) substance abuse counseling;</p>
          <p>(9) referral to a specialist, medical home, or hospital for care;</p>
          <p>(10) additional services that address social determinants of health; and</p>
          <p>(11) emerging services such as mobile health and telehealth.</p>
          <p>(b) Services provided by a school-based health center must not replace the daily student support provided in the school by educational student service providers, including but not limited to licensed school nurses, educational psychologists, school social workers, and school counselors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.903.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sponsoring organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>A sponsoring organization that agrees to operate a school-based health center must enter into a memorandum of agreement with the school or school district. The memorandum of agreement must require the sponsoring organization to be financially responsible for the operation of school-based health centers in the school or school district and must identify the costs that are the responsibility of the school or school district, such as Internet access, custodial services, utilities, and facility maintenance. To the greatest extent possible, a sponsoring organization must bill private insurers, medical assistance, and other public programs for services provided in the school-based health centers in order to maintain the financial sustainability of school-based health centers.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.903.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.59.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 59</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.905">
        <h1 class="shn">145.905 LOCATION FOR BREASTFEEDING.</h1>
        <p>A mother may breastfeed in any location, public or private, where the mother and child are otherwise authorized to be, irrespective of whether the nipple of the mother's breast is uncovered during or incidental to the breastfeeding.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.905.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.2.83.0">1998 c 407 art 2 s 83</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.906">
        <h1 class="shn">145.906 POSTPARTUM DEPRESSION EDUCATION AND INFORMATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of health shall work with health care facilities, licensed health and mental health care professionals, the women, infants, and children (WIC) program, mental health advocates, consumers, and families in the state to develop materials and information about postpartum depression, including treatment resources, and develop policies and procedures to comply with this section.</p>
        <p>(b) Physicians, traditional midwives, and other licensed health care professionals providing prenatal care to women must have available to women and their families information about postpartum depression.</p>
        <p>(c) Hospitals and other health care facilities in the state must provide departing new mothers and fathers and other family members, as appropriate, with written information about postpartum depression, including its symptoms, methods of coping with the illness, and treatment resources.</p>
        <p>(d) Information about postpartum depression, including its symptoms, potential impact on families, and treatment resources, must be available at WIC sites.</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner of health, in collaboration with the commissioner of human services and to the extent authorized by the federal Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, shall review the materials and information related to postpartum depression to determine their effectiveness in transmitting the information in a way that reduces racial health disparities as reported in surveys of maternal attitudes and experiences before, during, and after pregnancy, including those conducted by the commissioner of health. The commissioner shall implement changes to reduce racial health disparities in the information reviewed, as needed, and ensure that women of color are receiving the information.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.906.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/4/#laws.6.38.0">1Sp2005 c 4 art 6 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/247/#laws.2.8.0">2012 c 247 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.46.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.907">
        <h1 class="shn">145.907 MATERNAL DEPRESSION; DEFINITION.</h1>
        <p>"Maternal depression" means depression or other perinatal mood or anxiety disorder experienced by a woman during pregnancy or during the first year following the birth of her child.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.907.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.47.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 47</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.908">
        <h1 class="shn">145.908 GRANT PROGRAM; SCREENING AND TREATMENT FOR PRE- AND POSTPARTUM MOOD AND ANXIETY DISORDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.908.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p>Within the limits of available appropriations for this purpose, the commissioner of health shall establish a grant program to provide culturally competent programs to screen and treat pregnant women and women who have given birth in the preceding 12 months for pre- and postpartum mood and anxiety disorders. Organizations may use grant funds to establish new screening or treatment programs, or expand or maintain existing screening or treatment programs. In establishing the grant program, the commissioner shall prioritize expanding or enhancing screening for pre- and postpartum mood and anxiety disorders in primary care settings. The commissioner shall determine the types of organizations eligible for grants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.908.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Allowable uses of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Grant funds awarded by the commissioner under this section:</p>
          <p>(1) must be used to provide health care providers with appropriate training and relevant resources on screening, treatment, follow-up support, and links to community-based resources for pre- and postpartum mood and anxiety disorders; and</p>
          <p>(2) may be used to:</p>
          <p>(i) enable health care providers to provide or receive psychiatric consultations to treat eligible women for pre- and postpartum mood and anxiety disorders;</p>
          <p>(ii) conduct a public awareness campaign;</p>
          <p>(iii) fund start-up costs for telephone lines, websites, and other resources to collect and disseminate information about screening and treatment for pre- and postpartum mood and anxiety disorders; or</p>
          <p>(iv) establish connections between community-based resources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.908.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Federal funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall apply for any available grant funds from the federal Department of Health and Human Services for this program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.908.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.20.21.0">2016 c 189 art 20 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.11.68.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 11 s 68</a></p>
      </div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.915"><b>145.915</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.916"><b>145.916</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.917"><b>145.917</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.918"><b>145.918</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.919"><b>145.919</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.92"><b>145.92</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.921"><b>145.921</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.13">145A.13</a>]
</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.922"><b>145.922</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.27.0">1987 c 309 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.923"><b>145.923</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.14">145A.14, subd 3</a>]
</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">HEALTH GRANTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.924">
        <h1 class="shn">145.924 HIV PREVENTION GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner may award grants to community health boards as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.02#stat.145A.02.5">145A.02, subdivision 5</a>, state agencies, state councils, or nonprofit corporations to provide evaluation and counseling services to populations at risk for acquiring human immunodeficiency virus infection, including, but not limited to, communities of color, adolescents, women, people who inject drugs, and gay, bisexual, and transgender individuals.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner may award grants to agencies experienced in providing services to communities of color, for the design of innovative outreach and education programs for targeted groups within the community who may be at risk of acquiring the human immunodeficiency virus infection, including people who inject drugs and their partners, adolescents, women, and gay, bisexual, and transgender individuals. Grants shall be awarded on a request for proposal basis and shall include funds for administrative costs. Priority for grants shall be given to agencies or organizations that have experience in providing service to the particular community which the grantee proposes to serve; that have policy makers representative of the targeted population; that have experience in dealing with issues relating to HIV/AIDS; and that have the capacity to deal effectively with persons of differing sexual orientations. For purposes of this paragraph, the "communities of color" are: the American-Indian community; the Hispanic community; the African-American community; and the Asian-Pacific Islander community.</p>
        <p>(c) All state grants awarded under this section for programs targeted to adolescents shall include the promotion of abstinence from sexual activity and drug use.</p>
        <p>(d) The commissioner shall administer a grant program to provide funds to organizations, including Tribal health agencies, to assist with HIV outbreaks.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.924.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.24.0">1987 c 309 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/689/#laws.2.46.0">1988 c 689 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.2.31.0">1991 c 292 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.2.31.0">1999 c 245 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.109.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.60.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 60</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.9245"><b>145.9245</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.3.76.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 3 s 76</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.925">
        <h1 class="shn">145.925 SEXUAL AND REPRODUCTIVE HEALTH SERVICES GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.925.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Goal and establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) It is the goal of the state to increase access to sexual and reproductive health services for people who experience barriers, whether geographic, cultural, financial, or other, in access to such services. The commissioner of health shall administer grants to facilitate access to sexual and reproductive health services for people of reproductive age, particularly those from populations that experience barriers to these services.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of health shall coordinate with other efforts at the local, state, or national level to avoid duplication and promote complementary efforts in sexual and reproductive health service promotion among people of reproductive age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.925.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
