All relevant records and the performance of the grantee shall be monitored by the commissioner for purposes of assuring that the grantee continues to fulfill the criteria specified in subdivision 4. Should the commissioner at any time find that a grantee is not continuing to fulfill the criteria specified in subdivision 4, the commissioner may terminate the grant upon 30 days' notice.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.93.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/577/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 577 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/260/#laws.0.32.0">1983 c 260 s 32</a>,33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/223/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 223 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.2.1.0">1989 c 209 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.27.0">1999 c 159 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/43/#laws.0.20.0">2013 c 43 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE EXPOSURE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.94">
        <h1 class="shn">145.94 EXPOSURE TO HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.94.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Site inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>To determine hazardous substance exposure to the community, the commissioner of health may enter the premises of any employer as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/182.651#stat.182.651.7">182.651, subdivision 7</a>, including the University of Minnesota, to investigate the actual, suspected, or potential release of a hazardous substance if there is evidence or risk of exposure to the community. Before entering the commissioner shall present to the employer a statement of the reason, nature, and scope of the investigation at a particular location. As part of the investigation, and upon request to the employer, the commissioner must be allowed access to information required under the Employee Right-To-Know Act to determine if there are existing or potential health hazards to the community from the release of any hazardous substance originating in the workplace of the employer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.94.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disclosure of information.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may disclose to individuals or to the community, information including data made nonpublic by law, relating to the hazardous properties and health hazards of hazardous substances released from a workplace if the commissioner finds:</p>
          <p>(1) evidence that a person requesting the information may have suffered or is likely to suffer illness or injury from exposure to a hazardous substance; or</p>
          <p>(2) evidence of a community health risk and if the commissioner seeks to have the employer cease an activity which results in release of a hazardous substance.</p>
          <p>Nonpublic data obtained under subdivision 1 is subject to handling, use, and storage according to established standards to prevent unauthorized use or disclosure. If the nonpublic data is required for the diagnosis, treatment, or prevention of illness or injury, a personal physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant may be provided with this information if the physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant agrees to preserve the confidentiality of the information, except for patient health records subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.291">144.291</a> to 144.298. After the disclosure of any hazardous substance information relating to a particular workplace, the commissioner shall advise the employer of the information disclosed, the date of the disclosure, and the person who received the information.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/456/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 456 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.10.15.0">2007 c 147 art 10 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.66.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.77.0">2022 c 58 s 77</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.945">
        <h1 class="shn">145.945 CERTAIN SALES OF CLEANING PRODUCTS PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.945.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to prevent the spread of infectious disease and avoidable infections and to promote best practices in sanitation, no person shall offer for retail sale in Minnesota any cleaning product that contains triclosan and is used by consumers for sanitizing or hand and body cleansing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.945.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>The prohibition in subdivision 1 shall not apply to individual products for which specific United States Food and Drug Administration approval for consumer use has been secured.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.945.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/277/#laws.0.8.0">2014 c 277 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.95"><b>145.95</b> MS 1980 [Expired]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LONG-TERM DEVELOPMENT PROGRAM FOR CHILDREN</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.951">
        <h1 class="shn">145.951 IMPLEMENTATION PLAN; STATEWIDE PROGRAM FOR FAMILIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of health, in consultation with the commissioners of education; corrections; public safety; and human services, and with the director of the Council on Disability and the councils and commission under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.922">3.922</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.9221">3.9221</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0145">15.0145</a>, may develop an implementation plan for the establishment of a statewide program to assist families in developing the full potential of their children. The program must be designed to strengthen the family, to reduce the risk of abuse to children, and to promote the long-term development of children in their home environments. The program must also be designed to use volunteers to provide support to parents, and to link parents with existing public health, education, and social services as appropriate.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.951.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.25.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.87.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.95.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 95</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.952">
        <h1 class="shn">145.952 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.952.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.951">145.951</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.957">145.957</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.952.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Abuse.</span></h2>
          <p>"Abuse" means physical abuse, sexual abuse, neglect, mental injury, and threatened injury, as those terms are defined in chapter 260E.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.952.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">CHILD program or program.</span></h2>
          <p>"CHILD program" or "program" means the children helped in long-term development program that the commissioner shall plan to be implemented under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.951">145.951</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.957">145.957</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.952.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of health or the commissioner's designee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.952.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Local organization.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local organization" means an organization that contracts with the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.953#stat.145.953.1">145.953, subdivision 1</a>, to administer the CHILD program on a local level.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.952.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.26.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.30.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.953">
        <h1 class="shn">145.953 PROGRAM STRUCTURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.953.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Local administration of program.</span></h2>
          <p>The implementation plan must require the commissioner to contract with appropriate private nonprofit and governmental organizations to administer the CHILD program on a local level. The local organization, in collaboration and coordination with the Department of Health, shall be responsible for recruiting, screening training, and overseeing volunteers for the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.953.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Volunteer component.</span></h2>
          <p>The implementation plan must provide that a volunteer will be matched with a family to provide ongoing support in parenting. The volunteer shall provide the family with information on the CHILD program and other social services available. Through home visits and frequent contact, the volunteer shall provide support and guidance on raising the child and coping with stresses that may increase the risk of abuse. The volunteer shall also assist the family in obtaining other needed services from existing social services programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.953.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.27.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 27</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.954">
        <h1 class="shn">145.954 STANDARDS FOR PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>In planning for the implementation of the program, the commissioner shall:</p>
        <p>(1) establish mechanisms to encourage families to participate in the CHILD program;</p>
        <p>(2) establish mechanisms to identify families who may wish to participate in the CHILD program and to match volunteers with these families either before or as soon as possible after a child is born;</p>
        <p>(3) ensure that local organizations coordinate with services already provided by the Departments of Health, Human Services, and Education to ensure that participating families receive a continuum of care;</p>
        <p>(4) coordinate with local social services agencies, local health boards, and community health boards;</p>
        <p>(5) ensure that services provided through the program are community-based and that the special needs of minority communities are addressed;</p>
        <p>(6) develop and implement appropriate systems to gather data on participating families and to monitor and evaluate their progress; and</p>
        <p>(7) evaluate the program's effectiveness.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.954.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.28.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.955">
        <h1 class="shn">145.955 DUTIES OF LOCAL ORGANIZATION.</h1>
        <p>The implementation plan shall require the local organizations to:</p>
        <p>(1) recruit and train volunteers to serve families under the program, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.956">145.956</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) provide ongoing supervision and consultation to volunteers; and</p>
        <p>(3) develop resource and referral booklets that volunteers can distribute to families served by the program. The booklets shall contain comprehensive information on the spectrum of services available to assist the family and to reduce the risk of abuse.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.955.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.29.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 29</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.956">
        <h1 class="shn">145.956 TRAINING AND RECRUITMENT OF VOLUNTEERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.956.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Training requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The implementation plan shall require the local organization to carefully screen and train volunteers to provide program services. Training must prepare volunteers to:</p>
          <p>(1) identify signs of abuse or other indications that a child may be at risk of abuse;</p>
          <p>(2) help families develop communications skills;</p>
          <p>(3) teach and reinforce healthy discipline techniques;</p>
          <p>(4) provide other support a family needs to cope with stresses that increase the risk of abuse; and</p>
          <p>(5) refer the family to other appropriate public health, education, and social services.</p>
          <p>(b) The implementation plan shall also include procedures whereby the local agency will provide ongoing support, supervision, and training for all volunteers. Training must be culturally appropriate and community-based, and must incorporate input from parents who will be using the program's services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.956.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Recruitment of volunteers.</span></h2>
          <p>The implementation plan must require that the local organization recruit minority volunteers to serve communities of color.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.956.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.30.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 30</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.957">
        <h1 class="shn">145.957 ELIGIBILITY.</h1>
        <p>The implementation plan must ensure that all residents of Minnesota are eligible for services under the program. The plan must make services available on a sliding fee basis. The commissioner shall develop a sliding fee scale for the program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.957.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.31.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 31</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.9571">
        <h1 class="shn">145.9571 HEALTHY BEGINNINGS, HEALTHY FAMILIES ACT.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.9571">145.9571</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.9576">145.9576</a> are the Healthy Beginnings, Healthy Families Act.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.9571.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.64.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 64</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.9572">
        <h1 class="shn">145.9572 MINNESOTA PERINATAL QUALITY COLLABORATIVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9572.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota perinatal quality collaborative is established to improve pregnancy outcomes for pregnant people and newborns through efforts to:</p>
          <p>(1) advance evidence-based and evidence-informed clinics and other health service practices and processes through quality care review, chart audits, and continuous quality improvement initiatives that enable equitable outcomes;</p>
          <p>(2) review current data, trends, and research on best practices to inform and prioritize quality improvement initiatives;</p>
          <p>(3) identify methods that incorporate antiracism into individual practice and organizational guidelines in the delivery of perinatal health services;</p>
          <p>(4) support quality improvement initiatives to address substance use disorders in pregnant people and infants with neonatal abstinence syndrome or other effects of substance use;</p>
          <p>(5) provide a forum to discuss state-specific system and policy issues to guide quality improvement efforts that improve population-level perinatal outcomes;</p>
          <p>(6) reach providers and institutions in a multidisciplinary, collaborative, and coordinated effort across system organizations to reinforce a continuum of care model; and</p>
          <p>(7) support health care facilities in monitoring interventions through rapid data collection and applying system changes to provide improved care in perinatal health.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9572.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants authorized.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health must, within available appropriations, award one grant to a nonprofit organization to support efforts that improve maternal and infant health outcomes aligned with the purpose outlined in subdivision 1. The commissioner must give preference to a nonprofit organization that has the ability to provide these services throughout the state. The commissioner must provide content expertise to the grant recipient to further the accomplishment of the purpose.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.9572.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.65.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 65</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.9573">
        <h1 class="shn">145.9573 MINNESOTA PARTNERSHIP TO PREVENT INFANT MORTALITY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of health must establish the Minnesota partnership to prevent infant mortality program that is a statewide partnership program to engage communities, exchange best practices, share summary data on infant health, and promote policies to improve birth outcomes and eliminate preventable infant mortality.</p>
        <p>(b) The goal of the Minnesota partnership to prevent infant mortality program is to:</p>
        <p>(1) build a statewide multisectoral partnership including the state government, local public health agencies, Tribes, private sector, and community nonprofit organizations with the shared goal of decreasing infant mortality rates among populations with significant disparities, including among Black, American Indian, other nonwhite communities, and rural populations;</p>
        <p>(2) address the leading causes of poor infant health outcomes such as premature birth, infant sleep-related deaths, and congenital anomalies through strategies to change social and environmental determinants of health; and</p>
        <p>(3) promote the development, availability, and use of data-informed, community-driven strategies to improve infant health outcomes.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.9573.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.66.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 66</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.9574">
        <h1 class="shn">145.9574 GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9574.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Improving pregnancy and infant outcomes grant.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of health must, within available appropriations, make a grant to a nonprofit organization to create or sustain a multidisciplinary network of representatives of health care systems, health care providers, academic institutions, local and state agencies, and community partners that will collaboratively improve pregnancy and infant outcomes through evidence-based, population-level quality improvement initiatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9574.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Improving infant health grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health must award grants to eligible applicants to convene, coordinate, and implement data-driven strategies and culturally relevant activities to improve infant health by reducing preterm birth, sleep-related infant deaths, and congenital malformations and address social and environmental determinants of health. Eligible entities include community nonprofit organizations, Tribal governments, and community health boards. In accordance with available funding, the commissioner may award grants on a noncompetitive basis to the 11 sovereign Tribal governments if their respective proposals demonstrate the ability to implement programs designed to achieve the purposes in subdivision 1 and meet other requirements of this section. An eligible applicant must submit a complete application to the commissioner of health by the deadline established by the commissioner. The commissioner must award all other grants competitively to eligible applicants in metropolitan and rural areas of the state and may consider geographic representation in grant awards.</p>
          <p>(b) Grantee activities must:</p>
          <p>(1) address the leading cause or causes of infant mortality;</p>
          <p>(2) be based on community input;</p>
          <p>(3) focus on policy, systems, and environmental changes that support infant health; and</p>
          <p>(4) address the health disparities and inequities that are experienced in the grantee's community.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner must review each application to determine whether the application is complete and whether the applicant and the project are eligible for a grant. In evaluating applications according to this subdivision, the commissioner must establish criteria including but not limited to: the eligibility of the applicant's project under this section; the applicant's thoroughness and clarity in describing the infant health issues grant funds are intended to address; a description of the applicant's proposed project; the project's likelihood to achieve the grant's purposes as described in this section; a description of the population demographics and service area of the proposed project; and evidence of efficiencies and effectiveness gained through collaborative efforts.</p>
          <p>(d) Grant recipients must report their activities to the commissioner in a format and at a time specified by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.9574.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.67.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 67</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.9575">
        <h1 class="shn">145.9575 DEVELOPMENTAL AND SOCIAL-EMOTIONAL SCREENING WITH FOLLOW-UP.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9575.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Developmental and social-emotional screening with follow-up.</span></h2>
          <p>The goal of the developmental and social-emotional screening is to identify young children at risk for developmental and behavioral concerns and provide follow-up services to connect families and young children to appropriate community-based resources and programs. The commissioner of health must work with the commissioners of human services and education to implement this section and promote interagency coordination with other early childhood programs including those that provide screening and assessment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9575.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must:</p>
          <p>(1) increase the awareness of developmental and social-emotional screening with follow-up in coordination with community and state partners;</p>
          <p>(2) expand existing electronic screening systems to administer developmental and social-emotional screening to children from birth to kindergarten entrance;</p>
          <p>(3) provide screening for developmental and social-emotional delays based on current recommended best practices;</p>
          <p>(4) review and share the results of the screening with the parent or guardian and support families in their role as caregivers by providing anticipatory guidance around typical growth and development;</p>
          <p>(5) refer and connect children and families with appropriate community-based services and resources when any developmental or social-emotional concerns are identified through screening; and</p>
          <p>(6) establish performance measures and collect, analyze, and share program data regarding population-level outcomes of developmental and social-emotional screening, referrals to community-based services, and follow-up services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9575.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must award grants to support follow-up services for children with developmental or social-emotional concerns identified through screening in order to link children and their families to appropriate community-based services and resources. Grants may also be awarded to train and utilize cultural liaisons to help families navigate the screening and follow-up process in a culturally and linguistically responsive manner. Eligible grantees include community-based organizations, community health boards, and Tribal Nations. The commissioner must provide technical assistance, content expertise, and training to grant recipients to ensure that follow-up services are effectively provided.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.9575.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.68.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 68</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.9576">
        <h1 class="shn">145.9576 MODEL JAIL PRACTICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9576.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Model jail practices for incarcerated parents.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health may make grants to counties and groups of counties to implement model jail practices and to county governments, Tribal governments, or nonprofit organizations in corresponding geographic areas to build partnerships with county jails to support children of incarcerated parents and their caregivers.</p>
          <p>(b) "Model jail practices" means a set of practices that correctional administrators can implement to remove barriers that may prevent children from cultivating or maintaining relationships with their incarcerated parents during and immediately after incarceration without compromising the safety or security of the correctional facility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9576.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants authorized; model jail practices.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health may award grants to eligible county jails to implement model jail practices and separate grants to county governments, Tribal governments, or nonprofit organizations in corresponding geographic areas to build partnerships with county jails to support children of incarcerated parents and their caregivers.</p>
          <p>(b) Grantee activities include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) parenting classes or groups;</p>
          <p>(2) family-centered intake and assessment of inmate programs;</p>
          <p>(3) family notification, information, and communication strategies;</p>
          <p>(4) correctional staff training;</p>
          <p>(5) policies and practices for family visits; and</p>
          <p>(6) family-focused reentry planning.</p>
          <p>(c) Grant recipients must report their activities to the commissioner in a format and at a time specified by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.9576.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance and oversight; model jail practices.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner may provide content expertise, training to grant recipients, and advice on evidence-based strategies, including evidence-based training to support incarcerated parents.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of carrying out the grant program under subdivision 2, including for administrative purposes, the commissioner may award contracts to appropriate entities to assist in training and provide technical assistance to grantees.</p>
          <p>(c) Contracts awarded under paragraph (b) may be used to provide technical assistance and training in the areas of:</p>
          <p>(1) evidence-based training for incarcerated parents;</p>
          <p>(2) partnership building and community engagement;</p>
          <p>(3) evaluation of process and outcomes of model jail practices; and</p>
          <p>(4) expert guidance on reducing the harm caused to children of incarcerated parents and application of model jail practices.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.9576.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.69.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 69</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">YOUTH VIOLENCE PREVENTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.958">
        <h1 class="shn">145.958 YOUTH VIOLENCE PREVENTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.958.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "at-risk youth" means adolescents and teenagers who are likely to be a threat to the health and well-being of themselves or others through gang involvement, alcohol and drug use, unsafe sexual activity, dropping out of school, or through violence and other criminal activity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.958.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Violence prevention programs for at-risk youth.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Community-based violence prevention programs may apply to the commissioner of health for technical assistance. The programs must be community-based efforts serving at-risk youth and must work in collaboration with local schools, law enforcement agencies, faith communities, and community groups to provide a comprehensive approach to reducing youth violence by addressing the needs of at-risk youth.</p>
          <p>(b) The programs must:</p>
          <p>(1) ensure that there are trusted adults serving as role models and mentors for at-risk youth;</p>
          <p>(2) intervene at the first signs that a youth may be at risk and strive to rehabilitate youth who are already involved in violence;</p>
          <p>(3) work to strengthen families;</p>
          <p>(4) work with schools in order to keep students engaged and help them prepare for higher education or job training; and</p>
          <p>(5) teach self-respect and respect of others so that unsafe and unhealthy behaviors may be avoided.</p>
          <p>(c) Violence prevention programs may include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) mentorship;</p>
          <p>(2) job placement and support;</p>
          <p>(3) youth violence prevention training;</p>
          <p>(4) parent and family intervention and teaching parenting skills;</p>
          <p>(5) school-related initiative involving police liaison officers, youth leadership, peer mediation systems, after-school activities, and intervention in truancy cases;</p>
          <p>(6) substance use disorder and mental health intervention, screening, and assessment;</p>
          <p>(7) assisting juvenile offenders in reconnecting with families and reintegrating into the community;</p>
          <p>(8) working with youth to prevent sexual violence;</p>
          <p>(9) working with youth to prevent pregnancy and sexually transmitted infections; and</p>
          <p>(10) a youth helpline and street outreach workers to connect youth with needed services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.958.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Coordination of prevention and intervention for programs for at-risk youth.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of health, in collaboration with the commissioners of public safety, human services, and education, shall identify five community-based violence prevention programs that meet the criteria described in this section. One of these programs identified must be serving the youth in Minneapolis, one program must be serving the youth in St. Paul, and the remaining three programs must be serving youth in outstate communities.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of health shall provide technical support to these community programs including, but not limited to, assistance in seeking and applying for federal grants and private foundation funding.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of health shall monitor the progress of these programs in terms of the impact on public health and reducing juvenile violent crime and shall identify the effective aspects of each program in order to assist other programs in replicating these successful aspects.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner of health shall apply for private, state, or federal funding to support the activities described in this subdivision. This subdivision is effective upon the availability of funding to support these activities.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.958.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/156/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 156 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.145.97"><b>145.97</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/192/#laws.4.3.0">2014 c 192 art 4 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.145.98">
        <h1>145.98 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.98.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/192/#laws.4.3.0">2014 c 192 art 4 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.98.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/260/#laws.0.68.0">1983 c 260 s 68</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.98.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/192/#laws.4.3.0">2014 c 192 art 4 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.98.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/260/#laws.0.68.0">1983 c 260 s 68</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">HEALTH PROMOTION AND WELLNESS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.985">
        <h1 class="shn">145.985 HEALTH PROMOTION AND WELLNESS.</h1>
        <p class="in">Community health boards as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.02#stat.145A.02.5">145A.02, subdivision 5</a>, may work with schools, health care providers, and others to coordinate health and wellness programs in their communities. In order to meet the requirements of this section, community health boards may:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) provide instruction, technical assistance, and recommendations on how to evaluate project outcomes;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) assist with on-site health and wellness programs utilizing volunteers and others addressing health and wellness topics including smoking, nutrition, obesity, and others; and</p>
        <p class="in">(3) encourage health and wellness programs consistent with the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention's Community Guide and goals consistent with the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention's Healthy People 2010 initiative.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.985.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.15.14.0">2007 c 147 art 15 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.986">
        <h1 class="shn">145.986 STATEWIDE HEALTH IMPROVEMENT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of the statewide health improvement program is to:</p>
          <p>(1) address the leading preventable causes of illness and death such as tobacco use or exposure, poor diet, and lack of regular physical activity, and other issues as determined by the commissioner through the statewide health assessment;</p>
          <p>(2) promote the development, availability, and use of evidence-based, community level, comprehensive strategies to create healthy communities; and</p>
          <p>(3) measure the impact of the evidence-based, community health improvement practices which over time work to contain health care costs and reduce chronic diseases.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Grants to local communities.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner of health shall award competitive grants to community health boards and tribal governments to convene, coordinate, and implement proven-effective strategies, promising practice strategies, or theory-based strategies that can be evaluated using experimental or quasi-experimental design. Grants shall be awarded to all community health boards and tribal governments whose proposals demonstrate the ability to implement programs designed to achieve the purposes in subdivision 1 and other requirements of this section. In each grant cycle, the commissioner may award up to 100 percent of tribal grants and up to 25 percent of the grants awarded to community health boards to theory-based strategies that are culturally or ethnically focused.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Grantee activities shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) be based on scientific evidence;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) be based on community input;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) address behavior change at the individual, community, and systems levels;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) occur in community, school, work site, and health care settings;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) be focused on policy, systems, and environmental changes that support healthy behaviors; and</p>
          <p>(6) address the health disparities and inequities that exist in the grantee's community.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) To receive a grant under this section, community health boards and tribal governments must submit proposals to the commissioner. A local match of ten percent of the total funding allocation is required. This local match may include funds donated by community partners.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) In order to receive a grant, community health boards and tribal governments must submit a health improvement plan to the commissioner of health for approval. The commissioner may require the plan to identify a community leadership team, community partners, and a community action plan that includes an assessment of area strengths and needs, proposed action strategies, technical assistance needs, and a staffing plan.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The grant recipient must implement the health improvement plan, evaluate the effectiveness of the strategies, and modify or discontinue strategies found to be ineffective.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Grant recipients shall report their activities and their progress toward the outcomes established under subdivision 2 to the commissioner in a format and at a time specified by the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) All grant recipients shall be held accountable for making progress toward the measurable outcomes established in subdivision 2. The commissioner shall require a corrective action plan and may reduce the funding level of grant recipients that do not make adequate progress toward the measurable outcomes.</p>
          <p>(h) For purposes of this subdivision, "proven-effective" means a strategy or practice that offers a high level of research on effectiveness for at least one outcome of interest; "promising practice" means a practice or activity that is supported by research demonstrating effectiveness for at least one outcome of interest; and "theory-based" means a strategy or activity that has no research on effectiveness but has a well-constructed logical model or theory of change.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall set measurable outcomes to meet the goals specified in subdivision 1, and annually review the progress of grant recipients in meeting the outcomes.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner shall measure current public health status, using existing measures and data collection systems when available, to determine baseline data against which progress shall be monitored.</p>
          <p>(c) For grants awarded on or after July 1, 2016, the commissioner, in coordination with each grant recipient under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.986">145.986</a>, must identify:</p>
          <p>(1) each geographic area or population to be targeted;</p>
          <p>(2) the policy, systems, or environmental strategy to be used to address one or more of the health indicators listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62U.10#stat.62U.10.6">62U.10, subdivision 6</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) the selected outcomes and evaluation measures for the grant, related to one or more of the health indicators listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62U.10#stat.62U.10.6">62U.10, subdivision 6</a>, within the geographic area or among the population targeted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance and oversight.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall provide content expertise, technical expertise, training to grant recipients, and advice on evidence-based strategies, including those based on populations and types of communities served. The commissioner shall ensure that the statewide health improvement program meets the outcomes established under subdivision 2 by conducting a comprehensive statewide evaluation and assisting grant recipients to modify or discontinue interventions found to be ineffective.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of carrying out the grant program under this section, including for administrative purposes, the commissioner shall award contracts to appropriate entities to assist in training and provide technical assistance to grantees.</p>
          <p>(c) Contracts awarded under paragraph (b) may be used to provide technical assistance and training in the areas of:</p>
          <p>(1) community engagement and capacity building;</p>
          <p>(2) tribal support;</p>
          <p>(3) community asset building and risk behavior reduction;</p>
          <p>(4) legal;</p>
          <p>(5) communications;</p>
          <p>(6) community, school, health care, work site, and other site-specific strategies; and</p>
          <p>(7) health equity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Using the outcome measures established in subdivision 3, the commissioner shall conduct a biennial evaluation of the statewide health improvement program grants funded under this section. The evaluation must use the most appropriate experimental or quasi-experimental design suitable for the grant activity or project. Grant recipients shall cooperate with the commissioner in the evaluation and provide the commissioner with the information necessary to conduct the evaluation, including information on any impact on the health indicators listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62U.10#stat.62U.10.6">62U.10, subdivision 6</a>, within the geographic area or among the population targeted.</p>
          <p>(b) Grant recipients will collect, monitor, and submit to the Department of Health baseline and annual data and provide information to improve the quality and impact of community health improvement strategies.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purposes of carrying out the grant program under this section, including for administrative purposes, the commissioner shall award contracts to appropriate entities to assist in designing and implementing evaluation systems. The commissioner shall consult with the commissioner of management and budget to ensure that the evaluation process is using experimental or quasi-experimental design.</p>
          <p>(d) Contracts awarded under paragraph (c) may be used to:</p>
          <p>(1) develop grantee monitoring and reporting systems to track grantee progress, including aggregated and disaggregated data;</p>
          <p>(2) manage, analyze, and report program evaluation data results; and</p>
          <p>(3) utilize innovative support tools to analyze and predict the impact of prevention strategies on health outcomes and state health care costs over time.</p>
          <p>(e) For purposes of this subdivision, "experimental design" means a method of evaluating the impact of a strategy that uses random assignment to establish statistically similar groups, so that any difference in outcomes found at the end of the evaluation can be attributed to the strategy being evaluated; and "quasi-experimental design" means a method of evaluating the impact of a strategy that uses an approach other than random assignment to establish statistically similar groups, so that any difference in outcomes found at the end of the evaluation can be attributed to the strategy being evaluated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall submit a biennial report to the legislature on the statewide health improvement program funded under this section. The report must include information on each grant recipient, including the activities that were conducted by the grantee using grant funds, the grantee's progress toward achieving the measurable outcomes established under subdivision 2, and the data provided to the commissioner by the grantee to measure these outcomes for grant activities. The commissioner shall provide information on grants in which a corrective action plan was required under subdivision 1a, the types of plan action, and the progress that has been made toward meeting the measurable outcomes. In addition, the commissioner shall provide recommendations on future areas of focus for health improvement. These reports are due by January 15 of every other year, beginning in 2010. In the reports due beginning January 15, 2020, the commissioner shall include a description of the contracts awarded under subdivision 4, paragraph (c), and the monitoring and evaluation systems that were designed and implemented under these contracts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.986.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Supplantation of existing funds.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Community health boards and tribal governments must use funds received under this section to develop new programs, expand current programs, or replace discontinued state or federal funds. Funds must not be used to supplant current state or local funding to community health boards or tribal governments.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.986.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/358/#laws.1.1.0">2008 c 358 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.48.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.7.29.0">2014 c 291 art 7 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.8.47.0">2015 c 71 art 8 s 47</a>-49; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.10.98.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 10 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.11.71.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 11 s 71</a>-75</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.987">
        <h1 class="shn">145.987 HEALTH EQUITY ADVISORY AND LEADERSHIP (HEAL) COUNCIL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.987.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; composition of advisory council.</span></h2>
          <p>The health equity advisory and leadership (HEAL) council consists of 18 members appointed by the commissioner of health who will provide representation from the following groups:</p>
          <p>(1) African American and African heritage communities;</p>
          <p>(2) Asian American and Pacific Islander communities;</p>
          <p>(3) Latina/o/x communities;</p>
          <p>(4) American Indian communities and Tribal governments and nations;</p>
          <p>(5) disability communities;</p>
          <p>(6) lesbian, gay, bisexual, transgender, and queer (LGBTQ) communities; and</p>
          <p>(7) representatives who reside outside the seven-county metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.987.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Organization and meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Terms, compensation, and removal of members of the advisory council shall be as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059, subdivisions 2</a> to 4, except that terms for advisory council members shall be for two years. Members may be reappointed to serve up to two additional terms. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059#stat.15.059.6">15.059, subdivision 6</a>, the advisory council shall not expire. The commissioner shall recommend appointments to replace members vacating their positions in a timely manner, no more than three months after the advisory council reviews panel recommendations.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must convene meetings at least quarterly and must provide meeting space and administrative support to the advisory council. Subcommittees may be convened as necessary. Advisory council meetings are subject to the Open Meeting Law under chapter 13D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.987.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory council shall:</p>
          <p>(1) advise the commissioner on health equity issues and the health equity priorities and concerns of the populations specified in subdivision 1;</p>
          <p>(2) assist the agency in efforts to advance health equity, including consulting in specific agency policies and programs, providing ideas and input about potential budget and policy proposals, and recommending review of agency policies, standards, or procedures that may create or perpetuate health inequities; and</p>
          <p>(3) assist the agency in developing and monitoring meaningful performance measures related to advancing health equity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.987.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory council shall remain in existence until health inequities in the state are eliminated. Health inequities will be considered eliminated when race, ethnicity, income, gender, gender identity, geographic location, or other identity or social marker will no longer be predictors of health outcomes in the state. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.928">145.928</a> describes nine health disparities that must be considered when determining whether health inequities have been eliminated in the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.987.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15 each year, the commissioner or a designee, in collaboration with the advisory council, must submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over health policy and finance summarizing the work of the advisory council over the previous year and setting goals for the upcoming year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.987.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.70.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 70</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.145.988">
        <h1 class="shn">145.988 HELP ME CONNECT RESOURCE AND REFERRAL SYSTEM FOR CHILDREN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.988.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish the Help Me Connect resource and referral system for children as a comprehensive, collaborative resource and referral system for children from the prenatal stage through age eight, and their families. The commissioner of health shall work collaboratively with the commissioners of human services and education to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.145.988.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Help Me Connect system shall facilitate collaboration across sectors, including child health, early learning and education, child welfare, and family supports by:</p>
          <p>(1) providing early childhood provider outreach to support knowledge of and access to local resources that provide early detection and intervention services;</p>
          <p>(2) identifying and providing access to early childhood and family support navigation specialists that can support families and their children's needs; and</p>
          <p>(3) linking children and families to appropriate community-based services.</p>
          <p>(b) The Help Me Connect system shall provide community outreach that includes support for, and participation in, the Help Me Connect system, including disseminating information on the system and compiling and maintaining a current resource directory that includes but is not limited to primary and specialty medical care providers, early childhood education and child care programs, developmental disabilities assessment and intervention programs, mental health services, family and social support programs, child advocacy and legal services, public health services and resources, and other appropriate early childhood information.</p>
          <p>(c) The Help Me Connect system shall maintain a centralized access point for parents and professionals to obtain information, resources, and other support services.</p>
          <p>(d) The Help Me Connect system shall collect data to increase understanding of the current and ongoing system of support and resources for expectant families and children through age eight and their families, including identification of gaps in service, barriers to finding and receiving appropriate services, and lack of resources.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.145.988.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.71.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 71</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
